| [`test-budget`](#reliant-forge-ci-test-budget) | Fail when a test not marked slow took longer than the budget in a `go test -short -json` run (reads output; runs nothing) |
| [`validate-kcl`](#reliant-forge-ci-validate-kcl) | Validate that every environment renders manifests kubectl will accept |
| [`verify-generated`](#reliant-forge-ci-verify-generated) | Verify generated code is pristine and up to date |
| [`verify-test-run`](#reliant-forge-ci-verify-test-run) | Verify a `go test -json` run actually ran its tests (reads output; runs nothing) |
| [`vuln-scan`](#reliant-forge-ci-vuln-scan) | Run vulnerability scanners based on forge.yaml config |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge ci gate

Run the local CI gate: every check, one summary table, non-zero if any failed or was skipped

Runs the project's CI checks locally, exactly as the scaffolded workflow does.

Lanes (default: all):
  checks       verify-generated, lint, build, vuln-scan, license-check, KCL validation,
               deployability, and any ci.gate.extra_checks from forge.yaml   (`task ci:checks`)
  test         the unit suite, via `task test`
  integration  the integration suite, via `task test:integration`
  all          the three lanes, in one table                                   (`task ci`)
  `<lane>`       a lane extra checks of your own declare (`lane: docker`); never part of all

Four rules:
  1. Every check runs even if another failed; a summary table follows and the exit
     status is non-zero if any failed.
  2. A check that could not run (missing tool or prerequisite) is SKIPPED, not PASS,
     and fails the gate unless CI_SKIP names it: CI_SKIP=golangci-lint,license-check.
     The summary still lists it. N/A rows (nothing for the check to look at) are fine.
  3. Pinned tools (golangci-lint, buf, govulncheck, go-licenses) run at the versions the
     scaffolded workflow installs, from forge's tool cache, never from a different
     version that happens to be on PATH.
  4. Under GitHub Actions the table is appended to $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY.

Checks run concurrently. A check waits for the checks it names in `after`; a check that
rewrites the tree (an extra with `mutates_tree`) runs alone, before the checks that read
it (verify-generated regenerates a scratch copy, so it is not one); a heavy check holds
one of the machine's heavy slots (`forge slot status`), and at most --jobs light checks
run at once. Each check's output goes to
tmp/ci/`<lane>`/`<check>`.log; the console shows start and finish lines and a failure's tail.

On a committed tree, a check that PASSED on this exact tree before (any worktree of the
repository, same forge and pinned tools) is not run again: its row reads
`PASS (cached <sha>)`. CI_NO_CACHE=1 or --no-cache runs everything.

On a branch, unit-tests runs only the Go packages the branch's changes reach (affected
mode), and falls back to the whole suite when anything but Go source changed, on the
default branch, or with CI_FULL=1 / --full. The mode and why are printed.

Environment: CI_SKIP, CI_ONLY (checks or groups to run, with what they depend on),
CI_FULL, CI_BASE, CI_NO_CACHE, CI_REPORT (also write the Markdown table to a file),
CI_LOG_DIR, FORGE_CI_CACHE_DIR.

```
reliant forge ci gate [checks|test|integration|all|<lane>] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--base` | `string` | - | Ref a branch's changes are measured from for affected unit tests (default origin/\<default branch>; a stacked branch names its parent; also CI_BASE) |
| `--full` | `bool` | - | Run the whole unit suite on a branch instead of the affected packages (also CI_FULL=1) |
| `--groups` | `bool` | - | Print each check's output in a GitHub Actions ::group:: (automatic under GITHUB_ACTIONS) |
| `--jobs` | `int` | `0` | Light checks to run at once (default: half the CPUs, 2-8); heavy checks are bounded by host slots |
| `--list` | `bool` | - | Print the checks of the selected lanes (lane, group, name, scheduling) and exit |
| `--no-cache` | `bool` | - | Run every check even when this tree already passed it (also CI_NO_CACHE=1) |
| `--only` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Checks or groups to run, in addition to CI_ONLY (plus what they depend on) |
| `--report` | `string` | - | Also write the Markdown summary to this file (also CI_REPORT) |
| `--skip` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Check names to skip, in addition to CI_SKIP (still listed, as SKIPPED) |

---

#### reliant forge ci migration-safety

Run SQL migration safety checks based on forge.yaml config

Checks SQL migrations for patterns that pass on empty databases but fail or lock populated databases.

```
reliant forge ci migration-safety [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge ci run

Show one CI run's timeline — cut, checks, promotes, rollouts — and whether it passed

Read one CI run's timeline from the hosted control plane and say whether it
passed.

A run is everything one pipeline attempt wrote under its run id: the release it
cut, the checks it recorded (`forge gate record`), every promotion it made, and
each promotion's rollout. Nothing is a stored event — the control plane derives
the stages from the ledger on every read, so polling this is safe.

The run id is the one forge stamped on those writes: --run-id, or the CI
default (`github:<owner/repo>/<run>/<attempt>` under GitHub Actions).

WHICH CONTROL PLANE: --env names an env whose KCL declares it. Without --env,
the project's single declared control plane is used; with several, --env is
required. A run can span envs, so the env only chooses where to ask.

Exit codes (the hosted primitives' shared table):
  0  every stage passed (skipped stages count as passed)
  1  a stage failed or errored, or the request cannot be served (a run too
     large to read as one timeline)
  2  the control plane could not be read — unreachable, auth refused, or it
     does not serve run timelines
  5  a stage is still running: retry this command, the run has not finished

A run id nothing has written under is an EMPTY timeline and exits 5, never 0:
an id nobody used is not a pass. The control plane answers another
organisation's run id the same way, so this cannot be used to probe them.

Examples:
  forge ci run "github:acme/app/12345/1"
  forge ci run "$RUN_ID" --env prod --json | jq -r '.stages[] | select(.status != "passed")'

```
reliant forge ci run <run-id> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | - | Env whose declared control plane to ask (default: the project's only one) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit the timeline as JSON (same exit codes as text mode) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var and the credentials file |

---

#### reliant forge ci summarize

Render forge --json documents as a Markdown job summary (for $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY)

Render one or more forge --json documents as Markdown, for a CI job summary.

Every hosted deploy verb's --json carries the same envelope (ok, exit_code,
error); this prints that as a one-line verdict, the document's scalar fields as
a table, and its row lists (images, workloads, stages, promotions, gates) as
tables. It reads documents; it decides nothing.

It exits 0 whatever the documents say — the step that wrote each document
already exited with its verdict. It exits 1 only when an input cannot be read.

Examples:
  forge env deploy prod v1.4.0 --json > deploy.json
  forge env status prod --json > status.json
  forge ci summarize deploy.json status.json >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

```
reliant forge ci summarize <doc.json>... [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge ci test-budget

Fail when a test not marked slow took longer than the budget in a `go test -short -json` run (reads output; runs nothing)

Read a `go test -short -json` stream and report every top-level test that took
longer than --budget (default 2s), with its file and line.

  go test -short -json ./... | tee test.json | forge ci test-budget
  go test -short -json ./... > test.json; forge ci test-budget --from test.json

WHY. The -short tier is the blocking one, so every second in it is paid by
every change. A slow test belongs in the full tier, and says so:

  testkit.Slow(t, "builds the forge binary")      // forge/pkg/testkit
  if testing.Short() `{ t.Skip("builds the forge binary") }`

Under -short a marked test skips, so every test that RAN in a -short run claims
to be fast. One over budget is a slow test nobody marked. `forge lint
--slow-tests` flags slow work it can recognize in source; this catches the rest,
whatever made the test slow.

FEED IT A -short RUN. forge cannot see the flags a stream was made with. A test
whose own body checks testing.Short or calls testkit.Slow is reported as MARKED:
either it is still slow under -short (its bypass leaves slow work behind), or the
run was made without -short.

Top-level tests only: that is where the marker goes, and a table whose cases sum
past the budget is still that long in the blocking tier. A failing or unbuildable
package also fails the command, so piping a red run through it (in a shell
without `set -o pipefail`) cannot report green.

```
reliant forge ci test-budget [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--budget` | `duration` | `2s` | Longest a test not marked slow may take |
| `--from` | `string` | - | Read the 'go test -json' stream from this file instead of stdin |
| `--warn-only` | `bool` | - | Report overruns without failing (adoption ramp; unreadable input and test failures still fail) |

---

#### reliant forge ci validate-kcl

Validate that every environment renders manifests kubectl will accept

Renders each environment's deploy/kcl/`<env>`/main.k through the embedded KCL
runtime and asserts the result is DEPLOYABLE, not merely that it evaluates.

An env's applied stream is `output.manifests`, EXPANDED exactly as `forge env
deploy` expands it: every Cluster-bound forge.dev Workload record is rendered
through pkg/deploy.RenderWorkloads (Full profile), per (cluster, namespace)
set. A record that does not render fails here. Every expanded object must
carry apiVersion + kind, and no top-level key but `output` may hide k8s
objects no deploy would ever apply.

Hosting is PER WORKLOAD. The env's hosted part (forge.OnHosted workloads,
hosted databases, hosted frontends) is judged by its own deploy path:
each workload must pass Workload.Validate(ProfileRestricted), and the set must
render as the control plane renders it — the same plan `forge env deploy`
runs, minus the release and the RPCs.

Shares its implementation with `forge doctor --signal deploy`, so CI and
the doctor cannot disagree about whether a project can be deployed.

```
reliant forge ci validate-kcl [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge ci verify-generated

Verify generated code is pristine and up to date

Three checks of the working tree as it stands (tracked files plus untracked
files git does not ignore — on a clean checkout, HEAD):
  1. Self-certification: every generated file's embedded forge:hash marker
     must verify (recompute vs embedded) — catches hand-edits that were
     committed without --force / forge project disown.
  2. Freshness: runs forge generate and verifies no files changed —
     catches stale generated code after an input (proto/forge.yaml) change.
     The regenerate skips its steps when this exact tree, forge binary,
     toolset and environment were already verified as generate's own
     output (see `forge generate --help`); --no-cache runs them anyway.
  3. Commit policy: no generated file is gitignored (check 2 cannot see an
     ignored file), and no machine-local state (.forge-kcl/, a frontend's
     dev public/config.js) is tracked.

All three run against a scratch copy of the checkout, made beside the repository
(`.<repo>.forge-verify-*`) and removed afterwards, so the checkout itself is never
written: other readers of it can run at the same time, and an interrupted run
leaves nothing behind. Every change it reports was made by forge generate.

```
reliant forge ci verify-generated [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--no-cache` | `bool` | - | Make the regenerate run every step, even when the committed tree is one a full forge generate already verified as its own output (FORGE_GENERATE_NO_CACHE=1 does the same) |

---

#### reliant forge ci verify-test-run

Verify a `go test -json` run actually ran its tests (reads output; runs nothing)

Reads a `go test -json` stream and reports the packages that skipped so much
that their pass proves nothing.

This command RUNS NO TESTS. It reads the record of the run your project
already did, so it costs one extra flag and no extra time:

  go test -json ./... | tee test.json | forge ci verify-test-run
  go test -json ./... > test.json; forge ci verify-test-run --from test.json

`go test -json` swallows the human-readable output, hence the `tee` — keep the
raw stream for a human and hand a copy to forge.

TWO RULES, AND WHY NOT MORE. Skips are legitimate: `-short` exists, framework
limitations get documented skips, and one reference package keeps a genuine
unconditional skip even when fully configured. A gate that fires on every skip
is a gate that gets switched off. So:

  zero-evidence   every test in the package skipped — its "ok" is a statement
                  about nothing. No sample-size floor; unambiguous at any size.
  mass-skip       the package skipped more than --max-skip-ratio of its tests
                  and has at least --min-tests of them.

Healthy packages are never listed. When a package's heavy skipping is genuinely
expected — an integration-only package on a machine with no docker — declare it
once in forge.yaml, with a reason:

  ci:
    test_skips:
      allow:
        - package: internal/dockerintegration
          reason: "every test here needs a live docker daemon"

The reason is required and is read by humans, not by forge: an exemption nobody
had to justify is one nobody will revisit. A declaration that stops suppressing
anything is reported as no longer needed rather than left to rot.

WHICH RUN TO POINT IT AT. The one whose green you are treating as coverage.
A deliberately-reduced run (`-short`, a single package, a -run filter) is not a
claim about the whole suite, so gating it teaches people to ignore the gate;
forge cannot see the flags a stream was produced with and will not guess.

THREE STATES. Input that carries no `go test -json` events, or a stream that
ends mid-run, is UNDETERMINED — forge could not obtain the facts. That is not a
pass and it exits non-zero: this command never reports a clean run it did not
read. Failures in the stream also fail the command, because
`go test -json ./... | forge ci verify-test-run` in a shell without
`set -o pipefail` reports only the LAST command's status — a checker that
ignored them would launder a red suite green.

```
reliant forge ci verify-test-run [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | Read the 'go test -json' stream from this file instead of stdin |
| `--gate-json` | `string` | - | Also write this run's result to `FILE` as a gate document, for `forge gate record` or `forge env deploy --gate`. A FILE, not a stdout mode: the report and the exit code are unchanged. |
| `--max-skip-ratio` | `float64` | `0.5` | Share of a package's tests that may skip before it is reported |
| `--min-tests` | `int` | `5` | Sample-size floor for the mass-skip rule (a package that skipped EVERY test is reported regardless) |
| `--warn-only` | `bool` | - | Report skip findings without failing (adoption ramp; UNDETERMINED and test failures still fail) |

---

#### reliant forge ci vuln-scan

Run vulnerability scanners based on forge.yaml config

Runs govulncheck for Go and npm audit for frontends. Defaults to scanning everything
enabled in forge.yaml.

This is a GATE, so it never reports a pass it did not verify. If a selected scanner
cannot run — the binary is not on PATH, or the config selects no scanner at all —
the command FAILS and names the missing piece, rather than exiting 0 over a scan
that never happened. The success line names every scanner that actually ran.

Go fails on any advisory your code CALLS; npm fails on any advisory of severity
high or critical (lower ones are printed, not failed). An advisory with no fixed
version that cannot reach your code can be accepted under ci.vuln_scan.exemptions
in forge.yaml — by its GO-… id (govulncheck) or GHSA-… id (npm audit), with a
reason and an expiry date. Each entry accepts that one advisory and nothing else; an
expired or incomplete entry fails the gate, and one that matches nothing is reported
so it can be removed.

```
reliant forge ci vuln-scan [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--all` | `bool` | - | Run all scanners enabled in forge.yaml (default) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--go` | `bool` | - | Run govulncheck only |
| `--npm` | `bool` | - | Run npm audit only |

---

### reliant forge cloud

Talk to the hosted control plane an environment declares

Commands that reach the hosted control plane declared by an environment's
forge.ControlPlane block.

The endpoint is per-environment and comes from KCL, so which server a
command hits follows from its env argument — not from any stored
"current context".

Authenticate with `forge login`, or set the declared token env var.

```
reliant forge cloud [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`identity`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity) | Inspect managed identity and administer its users |
| [`status`](#reliant-forge-cloud-status) | Show the endpoint and credential source for an environment |
| [`token`](#reliant-forge-cloud-token) | Mint, list and revoke ORG automation tokens (the CI deploy credential) |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge cloud identity

Inspect managed identity and administer its users

Manage the identity an environment declares in KCL, using its control plane.

Reads (status, users) need identity:read; every verb that changes a user
(invite, set-roles, reset, lock, unlock, delete, revoke-sessions) and export
need identity:manage and an org admin. Under Reliant, or after forge login, nothing
extra is needed: when the control plane answers that the credential lacks one of
those scopes, forge trades it for the scope on demand (an hour, and only while
the person behind the session holds the grant), as deploy does for deploy:read.
A --token or the token env var is used exactly as given and must carry the scope.
The organization is resolved from the authenticated server, not a token claim or
a flag. The environment must already exist in this project.

--user takes a user id or an exact email address (see 'users').

Inviting creates the account (or grants roles to an existing one) and, for an
account with no credential yet, issues a one-time password-set code; reset
issues one for any account. The code is emailed only where the environment
declares smtp in KCL ('status' shows "Mail:"). Without smtp the control plane
refuses both rather than send nothing: pass --return-code to have the code
handed back, and deliver it yourself through a channel you control. The code is
a credential. There is no password option, seed account or implicit recipient.
Roles managed in deploy/kcl/identity.k (members_from = "declared") are
read-only here.

```
reliant forge cloud identity [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`delete`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-delete) | Delete a user's account (org admin; asks first) |
| [`export`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-export) | Export the identity's users now and print a short-lived download URL (identity:manage) |
| [`invite`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-invite) | Create an account (or grant roles) and issue a password-set code (org admin) |
| [`lock`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-lock) | Block a user from signing in (org admin) |
| [`reset`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-reset) | Issue a user a one-time password-set code (org admin) |
| [`revoke-sessions`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-revoke-sessions) | End a user's sign-in sessions (org admin) |
| [`set-roles`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-set-roles) | Replace a user's console-managed roles (org admin) |
| [`status`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-status) | Show managed-identity state, issuer and delivery metadata |
| [`unlock`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-unlock) | Let a locked user sign in again (org admin) |
| [`users`](#reliant-forge-cloud-identity-users) | List one page of users and declared roles (no credentials) |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge cloud identity delete

Delete a user's account (org admin; asks first)

Remove the account. This cannot be undone. A user who holds a declared role is
refused: the next reconcile would recreate them. Without --yes the command asks
on the terminal and refuses when stdin is not one.

```
reliant forge cloud identity delete <env> --user <id|email> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |
| `--user` | `string` | - | The user, by user id or exact email address (required) |
| `--yes`, `-y` | `bool` | - | Delete without asking |

---

#### reliant forge cloud identity export

Export the identity's users now and print a short-lived download URL (identity:manage)

Ask the control plane to export the environment's identity instance — every
user, password hashes included, in Zitadel's import format — and print a signed
download URL valid for minutes. Requests within the same 15 minutes share one
export. For a withdrawn identity the URL is for the export written when it was
withdrawn.

The URL is a credential: anyone holding it can download every user's password
hash until it expires. It is printed once and never logged; do not paste it
into a ticket or a chat. Needs identity:manage and, for a person, an org admin
holding the identity:manage grant. Refused where export is not enabled for the
control plane, or the instance is not ready.

```
reliant forge cloud identity export <env> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |

---

#### reliant forge cloud identity invite

Create an account (or grant roles) and issue a password-set code (org admin)

Invite this explicit email address; repeat --role for multiple roles.
An existing account receives the requested roles (created=false). An account
with no credential yet is issued a one-time password-set code: emailed where
the environment declares smtp, or, with --return-code, printed here for you to
deliver. Without smtp and without --return-code the control plane refuses the
invitation before writing anything. The server also refuses unknown or
declared/read-only roles, a credential without identity:manage and an identity
that is not ready. A refusal is an error, never a fallback to a seeded password.

```
reliant forge cloud identity invite <env> --email <address> --role <role> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--email` | `string` | - | Explicit recipient email address (required; never inferred) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--return-code` | `bool` | - | Print the password-set code instead of emailing it (it is a credential; needed where the environment declares no smtp) |
| `--role` | `stringArray` | `[]` | Console-managed role to grant (required; repeat for multiple roles) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |

---

#### reliant forge cloud identity lock

Block a user from signing in (org admin)

Block sign-in for the user. Reversible with `forge cloud identity unlock`.

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |

| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |

#### reliant forge cloud identity reset

Issue a user a one-time password-set code (org admin)

Issue the user a one-time password-set code. It is emailed as a reset link
where the environment declares smtp; with --return-code it is printed here
instead, for you to deliver over a channel you control (it is a credential).
Without smtp ('status' shows "Mail: none") the control plane refuses a reset
without --return-code rather than send nothing. The user's current password
keeps working until the code is redeemed.

```
reliant forge cloud identity reset <env> --user <id|email> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--return-code` | `bool` | - | Print the password-set code instead of emailing it (it is a credential; needed where the environment declares no smtp) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |
| `--user` | `string` | - | The user, by user id or exact email address (required) |

#### reliant forge cloud identity set-roles

Replace a user's console-managed roles (org admin)

Replace the user's console-managed roles with exactly the --role values. Roles
the environment declares in deploy/kcl/identity.k are left as they are, and
naming one is refused. To remove every console-managed role, pass --none.

```
reliant forge cloud identity set-roles <env> --user <id|email> --role <role> [flags]
```

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|

| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--none` | `bool` | - | Remove every console-managed role |
| `--role` | `stringArray` | `[]` | Console-managed role the user should hold (repeat for several) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |
| `--user` | `string` | - | The user, by user id or exact email address (required) |

|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON (never credentials) |
| `--page-size` | `int` | `20` | Maximum users in this page (0 uses server default, max 100) |
| `--page-token` | `string` | - | Continuation from the preceding response |
| `--query` | `string` | - | Case-insensitive email substring (max 200 characters) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var, stored login and session helper |

#### reliant forge cloud token

Mint, list and revoke ORG automation tokens (the CI deploy credential)

Manage the organization's automation tokens on the hosted control plane.

An org token has no acting user, so it outlives any one person — which is what
a CI pipeline's FORGE_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN must be. Minting needs a human session
(an org admin, via `forge login`); the control plane refuses a machine credential.

Typical setup for the scaffolded release workflow:

  forge cloud token create --env prod --name github-actions \
      --scopes deploy:read,deploy:write --json | jq -r .secret \
    | gh secret set FORGE_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN

```
reliant forge cloud token [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`create`](#reliant-forge-cloud-token-create) | Mint an org automation token; its secret is printed ONCE |
| [`list`](#reliant-forge-cloud-token-list) | List the org's automation tokens (never their secrets) |
| [`revoke`](#reliant-forge-cloud-token-revoke) | Revoke an org automation token, immediately |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---


|------|------|---------|-------------|

| `--env` | `string` | - | Env whose declared control plane to use (required) |
| `--expires-in` | `duration` | `0s` | Expire after this long (default: never) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON |
| `--name` | `string` | - | What the token is for, e.g. github-actions (required) |
| `--org` | `string` | - | Explicit organization for token administration (not a login or project preference) |
| `--scopes` | `string` | - | Comma-separated scopes, e.g. deploy:read,deploy:write (required) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var and the credentials file |

### reliant forge cluster

Manage the local k3d cluster and inspect dev state

Manage the local k3d development cluster and inspect dev-loop state.

forge cluster owns the universal mechanics every k8s-targeting forge
project needs: k3d cluster lifecycle, ingress URLs, status, logs.
Project-specific orchestration (sibling-repo deploys, helm chart
bootstraps, webhook listeners) lives in your scripts/ and Taskfile.yml —
composed with the forge cluster primitives.

The cluster config is read from deploy/k3d.yaml (override via --config).
Lifecycle subcommands pin the kubectl context to k3d-`<cluster-name>` as a
guardrail against accidental prod-context leaks.

Examples:
  forge cluster up             # create k3d cluster from deploy/k3d.yaml
  forge cluster reload         # re-render KCL + kubectl apply + wait rollout
  forge cluster urls           # print the ingress URL table for the env
  forge cluster status         # cluster + pods + ingress URLs
  forge cluster logs --service api  # kubectl logs -f for a service
  forge cluster instances      # list every forge dev namespace on the host

  forge cluster connect prod-us --context gke_acme_us-central1_prod --env prod
  forge cluster disconnect prod-us --env prod   # a cluster YOU operate, as a deploy target
  forge cluster list --env prod                 # what the control plane holds for them
  forge cluster show prod-us --env prod --json

```
reliant forge cluster [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`connect`](#reliant-forge-cluster-connect) | Register a Kubernetes cluster the control plane may deploy into, as one env's binding |
| [`disconnect`](#reliant-forge-cluster-disconnect) | Deregister a connected cluster and remove every grant forge created in it |
| [`down`](#reliant-forge-cluster-down) | Delete the k3d cluster |
| [`info`](#reliant-forge-cluster-info) | Print static dev-loop config (cluster name, expected context, declared ports) |
| [`instances`](#reliant-forge-cluster-instances) | List every forge dev namespace on every reachable cluster |
| [`list`](#reliant-forge-cluster-list) | List the clusters connected to the control plane, with their state and env bindings |
| [`logs`](#reliant-forge-cluster-logs) | Stream kubectl logs for one or all services in the dev namespace |
| [`recover-chart-lock`](#reliant-forge-cluster-recover-chart-lock) | Explicitly recover an inspected chart lock after terminating its writer |
| [`reload`](#reliant-forge-cluster-reload) | Re-render deploy/kcl/dev + kubectl apply + wait rollout |
| [`reset`](#reliant-forge-cluster-reset) | Delete then recreate the cluster |
| [`show`](#reliant-forge-cluster-show) | Show one connected cluster |
| [`status`](#reliant-forge-cluster-status) | Print dynamic dev-loop state (cluster up/down, pods, ingress URLs) |
| [`up`](#reliant-forge-cluster-up) | Create the k3d cluster from deploy/k3d.yaml |
| [`urls`](#reliant-forge-cluster-urls) | Print the ingress URL table for the dev env |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge cluster connect

Register a Kubernetes cluster the control plane may deploy into, as one env's binding

Register a cluster you operate, by address, and grant ONE env's binding exactly
what the control plane applies there.

  forge cluster connect prod-us --context gke_acme_us-central1_prod --env prod
  forge cluster connect prod-us --context ... --env prod --apply-scope namespace:acme-prod,acme-jobs
  forge cluster connect hub --context ... --env prod --apply-scope cluster
  forge cluster connect prod-us --context ... --env prod --address https://203.0.113.10:6443 --ca-file ca.pem
  forge cluster connect prod-us --context ... --env prod --dry-run
  forge cluster disconnect prod-us --env prod --context ...

HOW IT AUTHENTICATES — one way, on any Kubernetes cluster. Your own kube context
(--context) bootstraps a dedicated ServiceAccount for this binding and applies
its least-privilege RBAC; forge reads that ServiceAccount's token and uploads it,
write-only, to the control plane. The control plane authenticates to your cluster
as that ServiceAccount and nothing else: no cloud identity of ours is trusted in
your project, and there is no IAM grant for you to run. The token is bounded by
the RBAC below and revoked by disconnect (which deletes the ServiceAccount).
Rotation is a re-run of connect.

A BINDING is one env on one cluster, with its OWN ServiceAccount. Nothing is
shared across bindings. The only objects connect writes in your cluster are the
grant itself, that ServiceAccount and its token Secret.

ADDRESS — the API server the control plane dials, from its own pods:

  default        the context's cluster.server and CA. On a GKE context, the
                 cluster's PUBLIC endpoint and CA from GKE (gcloud describe).
  --address      an https://host[:port] override, port 443 or 6443. Use it when
                 the control plane reaches the cluster at another address than
                 your kubeconfig does.
  --ca-file      a PEM bundle for the API server's certificate, over the CA
                 forge would otherwise read.

A GKE DNS endpoint (*.gke.goog) is refused: it authenticates Google identities,
and a ServiceAccount token cannot pass it. Private, VPC, link-local and metadata
addresses are refused by the control plane unless your organization is exempt.

APPLY SCOPE — YOU choose where the identity may write; the env's render
chooses what, and only for what the CONTROL PLANE applies:

  namespace:`<ns>`[,`<ns>`…]   a Role and RoleBinding in each named namespace. The
                           DEFAULT, over every namespace the render targets.
                           Refused if the render carries cluster-scoped kinds.
  cluster                  a ClusterRole and ClusterRoleBinding over every
                           namespace, and forge says so every time.

Either way the rules are derived from the render: one rule per resource the
control plane applies, nothing else. Delivery units that 'forge env deploy'
applies itself (bundle.applier = "forge", or units left out of
bundle.hub_converges) contribute nothing: an env where forge applies everything
gets NO apply rules, and connect says so. escalate and bind are never granted
without resourceNames: where the render carries Roles or ClusterRoles they are
pinned to those exact names, and impersonate is never granted. A release that
adds a kind is Forbidden by your cluster's own access check until you re-run
connect — review it first with --dry-run, which prints the exact RBAC diff
against what is on the cluster.

Re-running connect UPDATES the cluster of that name and prunes grants the new
plan no longer names. --env names which control plane to talk to AND whose
binding this is; the render is read from the project in the current directory.

DRY RUN — contacts no control plane and changes nothing. It renders the env,
reads the binding's live RBAC through --context (read-only) — or from a capture
with --live-from `<file>` (kubectl get clusterrole,clusterrolebinding,role,
rolebinding -A -l `<selector>` -o yaml, selector printed by --dry-run) — and
prints the diff and the permissions this run would newly grant.

SECRET DELIVERY — opt-in, off unless named. A Secret the control plane supplies
and renews (forge.ControlPlaneSecret) needs write access to exactly that
Secret and no other. Name each one:

  forge cluster connect prod-us --context ... --env prod \
    --delivery-secret control-plane-prod/reliant-otel-auth

forge then applies, per namespace, a Role limited by resourceNames to get,
update, patch and delete on the named Secrets, plus create — which RBAC cannot
limit by name, so a ValidatingAdmissionPolicy admits only the named
namespace/name pairs, only as type Opaque, for the delivery identities only.
--delivery-subject (user:`<name>` or serviceaccount:`<ns>`/`<name>`) binds a second,
dedicated delivery identity alongside the binding's identity.

SECRET-STORE ISSUER — connect also tells the control plane which service-account
issuer the cluster's pods are signed by, so the secret store can verify the
projected token an External Secrets Operator logs in with, against the cluster's
OWN keys (no stored credential, no call back into the cluster). forge reads the
issuer and key set from the API server through --context:

  --issuer auto      (default) discover on GKE, upload elsewhere; if the issuer
                     cannot be read or the control plane refuses it, the cluster
                     still connects for deploys and forge says what is missing
  --issuer discover  the store fetches `<issuer>`/.well-known/openid-configuration
                     itself; the issuer must be reachable from the internet
  --issuer upload    forge sends /openid/v1/jwks: for a cluster the store cannot
                     reach (private, k3d). PUBLIC keys only; a set holding a
                     private or symmetric key is never sent. A snapshot: re-run
                     connect after a signing-key rotation to refresh it
  --issuer skip      leave the registration as it is
  --issuer withdraw  remove it; the store stops trusting the cluster

The binding's grant gains 'get' on /openid/v1/jwks. The store's id for the
cluster (its auth mount is jwt-`<id>`) is assigned by the control plane, is global
across organizations, and is printed and kept in
.forge/state/cluster-issuer-`<name>`.json; use it as the connected cluster name.

```
reliant forge cluster connect <name> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--address` | `string` | - | https://host[:port] the control plane dials, port 443 or 6443 (default: the context's server; on GKE the cluster's public endpoint) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--apply-scope` | `string` | - | Where the binding's identity may write: namespace:`<ns>`[,`<ns>`…] (default: the namespaces the env renders) or cluster (cluster-admin equivalent) |
| `--ca-file` | `string` | - | PEM file for the API server's certificate authority (default: read from the context, or GKE) |
| `--context` | `string` | - | kubectl context addressing the cluster (required) |
| `--delivery-secret` | `stringArray` | `[]` | `<namespace>`/`<name>` of a Secret the control plane may write and renew (repeatable) |
| `--delivery-subject` | `string` | - | Also bind the delivery grant to this dedicated identity: user:`<name>` or serviceaccount:`<namespace>`/`<name>` |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print the RBAC diff against the cluster and what would be sent; contact no control plane and change nothing |
| `--env` | `string` | - | Environment whose binding this is, and whose control plane to talk to (required) |
| `--issuer` | `string` | `auto` | Register the cluster's service-account issuer with the secret store: auto, discover, upload, skip or withdraw |
| `--live-from` | `string` | - | With --dry-run: diff against this kubectl capture instead of reading the cluster |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var and the credentials file |

---

#### reliant forge cluster disconnect

Deregister a connected cluster and remove every grant forge created in it

Deregister the connected cluster of this exact name.

Two halves, and the order matters: the control plane revokes the cluster's
credential first, then forge deletes the grant it created in the cluster — the
Roles, ClusterRoles and bindings of every binding of this cluster, each
binding's token ServiceAccount and Secret, and the delivery policy. They are
found by label, so nothing else in the cluster is touched. A credential revoked
server-side is already powerless, so a failure to reach the cluster afterwards
leaves litter rather than a live credential.

REFUSED while any live environment targets the cluster — that check is the
control plane's, and it is why this is not a local delete.

  forge cluster disconnect prod-us --env prod --context gke_acme_us-central1_prod

--context is optional and names where to clean up. Without it, forge
deregisters the cluster and prints the commands to delete the grant by hand.

```
reliant forge cluster disconnect <name> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--context` | `string` | - | kubectl context to delete forge's objects through |
| `--env` | `string` | - | Environment whose control plane to talk to (required) |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var and the credentials file |

---

#### reliant forge cluster down

Delete the k3d cluster

Delete the k3d cluster named by --config's metadata.name, and FIRST every
cluster nested on its docker network (declared with `owner`, so it has no
config file of its own and cannot outlive its owner's network).

Given an environment, delete every k3d cluster its KCL declares instead —
including secondaries declared with `owner` and no config file, which
--config cannot name. Secondaries are deleted before their owner: k3d cannot
remove a docker network a secondary is still attached to.

This deletes whole clusters, and with them every namespace on them — including
other environments' and other worktrees' stacks sharing the cluster.

```
reliant forge cluster down [environment] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file (ignored when an environment is given) |

---

#### reliant forge cluster info

Print static dev-loop config (cluster name, expected context, declared ports)

Print the static dev-loop config declared in forge.yaml + deploy/k3d.yaml.

Static means "what the project says it expects" — cluster name, expected
kubectl context, registry URL, declared service/frontend ports. It does
NOT contact the cluster or check pod state.

For dynamic state (is the cluster up? are pods running? what are the
live ingress URLs?) use `forge cluster status`.

```
reliant forge cluster info [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file |

#### reliant forge cluster instances

List every forge dev namespace on every reachable cluster

List every forge-managed dev namespace on the host.

Inspects each k3d cluster's kubeconfig context and reports the
namespaces labelled app.kubernetes.io/managed-by=forge. Useful for the
multi-worktree workflow: many worktrees, each with its own namespace,
all sharing one cluster (or one per worktree).

Examples:
  forge cluster instances
  forge cluster instances --json

```
reliant forge cluster instances [flags]
```

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Emit machine-readable JSON |

#### reliant forge cluster list

List the clusters connected to the control plane, with their state and env bindings

List every cluster the organization has connected, as the control plane sees it:
name, id, state, auth, registered address and CA fingerprint, the secret-store
issuer registration, the environments bound to it, and created/updated times.

  forge cluster list --env prod
  forge cluster list --env prod --json

State is the control plane's own: CONNECTED (it holds usable connection
material), WAITING (it holds nothing to try with) or STALE (the token's expiry
has passed). Use it to verify a 'forge cluster connect'.

--json prints one document, schema forge.cluster.list/v1. A member the control plane did not
report is null. No token or certificate is ever printed.

--env names which control plane to talk to (required).

```
reliant forge cluster list [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | - | Environment whose control plane to talk to (required) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print one machine-readable document |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Credential to use, ahead of the env var and the credentials file |

---

#### reliant forge cluster logs

Stream kubectl logs for one or all services in the dev namespace

Stream kubectl logs.

Without --service, streams logs from every pod managed by forge in the
dev namespace (the same label forge uses to discover deployments).
With --service, scopes to one service deployment.

Examples:
  forge cluster logs                       # tail every forge-managed pod
  forge cluster logs --service api         # tail one service
  forge cluster logs --service api --tail 200
  forge cluster logs --no-follow           # one-shot, no streaming

```
reliant forge cluster logs [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file |
| `--follow` | `bool` | `true` | Stream new logs (kubectl logs -f) |
| `--service` | `string` | - | Scope to one service deployment |
| `--tail` | `int` | `100` | Lines of recent log file to display (-1 = all) |

#### reliant forge cluster recover-chart-lock

Explicitly recover an inspected chart lock after terminating its writer

A chart lock never expires. Before recovery, terminate/fence the holder's
forge process AND its kubectl children on every host; ensure outstanding API
writes have completed or their credentials have been revoked. A suspended writer
must never resume after recovery. Do not recover a merely slow writer.

Inspect the ConfigMap named in the deploy error in kube-system. This command
requires its owner, chart, UID, resourceVersion and literal terminated:`<holder>`
confirmation. It conditionally deletes ONLY that lock, never the inventory.
There is no --force, timeout-based takeover, or automatic unlock on failure.

```
reliant forge cluster recover-chart-lock [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--chart` | `string` | - | Inspected chart name |
| `--confirm-terminated` | `string` | - | Explicit terminated:`<holder>` attestation after fencing the old writer |
| `--context` | `string` | - | Explicit kubectl context |
| `--owner` | `string` | - | Inspected forge project.env owner |
| `--resource-version` | `string` | - | Inspected lock metadata.resourceVersion |
| `--uid` | `string` | - | Inspected lock metadata.uid |

---

#### reliant forge cluster reload

Re-render deploy/kcl/dev + kubectl apply + wait rollout

Re-render the dev KCL manifests, apply, and wait for rollout.

This is the inner loop during local development: after editing code or
KCL, run this to push the change into the cluster without rebuilding the
docker image (the same code path forge env deploy dev uses, but skips the
cluster bootstrap).

Examples:
  forge cluster reload
  forge cluster reload --image-tag dev-2026-06-01
  forge cluster reload --dry-run

```
reliant forge cluster reload [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print manifests without applying |
| `--image-tag` | `string` | - | Image tag (default: git short SHA) |
| `--namespace` | `string` | - | Override namespace from environment config |

#### reliant forge cluster status

Print dynamic dev-loop state (cluster up/down, pods, ingress URLs)

Print the dynamic state of the local dev environment.

Dynamic means "what's actually happening right now" — does the k3d
cluster exist, what's the current kubectl context, what pods are in
the dev namespace, what ingress URLs are exposed by the dev env's
KCL gateways, what sibling dev namespaces exist on this cluster.

For static config (declared cluster name, expected context, declared
service/frontend ports) run `forge cluster info`.

Examples:
  forge cluster status
  forge cluster status --json    # machine-readable for scripts/dashboards

```
reliant forge cluster status [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file |

#### reliant forge cluster up

Create the k3d cluster from deploy/k3d.yaml

Create the k3d cluster from deploy/k3d.yaml.

If the cluster already exists, this is a no-op success. With --wait,
blocks until the cluster's nodes report ready.

Given an environment, the clusters come from that environment's KCL instead
of a k3d config file: every forge.Cluster the env's bundle declares is ensured exactly
as `forge env up <env>` ensures it — the declared pod/Service CIDRs, API port,
owner network and registry-inherit included, none of which a k3d YAML carries.
Use it whenever the env declares its clusters: a cluster created from the bare
config file lacks those fields, and the env's own deploy then refuses it.

Examples:
  forge cluster up
  forge cluster up --wait
  forge cluster up --config deploy/k3d.custom.yaml
  forge cluster up dev-k8s --wait

```
reliant forge cluster up [environment] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--config` | `string` | `deploy/k3d.yaml` | k3d config file (ignored when an environment is given) |
| `--wait` | `bool` | - | Wait until cluster nodes are ready |

When features.ingress is disabled this prints a short notice and

### reliant forge db

Database and migration commands

Manage database migrations.

Forge uses a migration-first database model:
- Checked-in SQL migrations in db/migrations/ are the source of truth
- forge's migrator (pkg/migratekit, on golang-migrate) applies them, from
  'forge db migrate' and from the project's own binary alike
- Entity types are projections of the applied schema (forge generate)

To read an environment's data: 'forge db query `<env>` "`<sql>`"' runs one
read-only statement and prints the rows.

When something is wedged:

  A migration failed part-way and the state is marked dirty
    No further migration will run until the flag clears, and 'migrate up'
    refuses too. Repair the schema by hand, then:
      forge db migrate force `<version>`   # record it as applied, runs no SQL
      forge db migrate up
    Or, on a scratch dev database, skip the repair entirely:
      forge db reset                     # DROP, recreate, migrate, seed (dev-only)

  A migration cannot apply because existing rows violate it
    Adding a constraint to a column seeding filled with placeholders wedges
    both repairs against each other: 'seed reset' refuses because the schema
    is behind, 'migrate up' refuses because of the rows. Discard the state:
      forge db reset                     # needs no dirty-state reasoning (dev-only)

  The dev database is full of bad or stale rows
    Do not drop the database by hand:
      forge db seed reset                # delete seeded rows and re-seed (dev-only)
      forge db reset                     # or rebuild the whole database (dev-only)

  Seeded rows are rejected by their own schema
    'forge db seed apply' names the constraint it could not place, and why.
    Load the db/seeding skill for the constraint shapes forge can seed.

Project files (db/migrations, db/seeds/vocab.yaml, db/seeds/custom/) are read
from the project root: the one -C names, or the one the current directory is
in. A relative --dir resolves against that root too.

```
reliant forge db [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`introspect`](#reliant-forge-db-introspect) | Inspect the migrated database schema |
| [`migrate`](#reliant-forge-db-migrate) | Apply, inspect and repair migration state with forge's migrator |
| [`migration`](#reliant-forge-db-migration) | Create and re-version forward-only SQL migration files |
| [`query`](#reliant-forge-db-query) | Run one read-only SQL statement against an environment's app database |
| [`reset`](#reliant-forge-db-reset) | DROP the dev database, recreate it, migrate to head, and seed (dev-only) |
| [`seed`](#reliant-forge-db-seed) | Materialize deterministic development seed data at runtime |
| [`squash`](#reliant-forge-db-squash) | Collapse N migrations into one canonical baseline (.up.sql) |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

Connect to a PostgreSQL database and display the current schema.

Shows tables, columns, types, constraints, indexes, and foreign keys.

Examples:
  forge db introspect --dsn "postgres://user:pass@localhost/mydb?sslmode=disable"
  forge db introspect --dsn "$DATABASE_URL" --table users
  forge db introspect --dsn "$DATABASE_URL" --format json

```
reliant forge db introspect [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dsn` | `string` | - | PostgreSQL connection string (required) |
| `--format` | `string` | `text` | Output format: text, json |
| `--table` | `string` | - | Filter to a specific table |

#### reliant forge db migrate

Apply, inspect and repair migration state with forge's migrator

Apply, inspect, and repair migration state with forge's migrator
(forge/pkg/migratekit, built on golang-migrate) — the same one the project's
own '`<binary>` db migrate up' and a server's AUTO_MIGRATE run, so every path
takes the same run lock, keeps the same applied record, and reaches the same
verdicts:

  - a version applied from a different file, or one the schema passed without
    applying, is refused before anything runs;
  - a database goose migrated is ADOPTED when the first migration declares
    its history ('-- forge:adopts goose ...');
  - a schema AHEAD of the directory is reported, and refused unless every
    newer version was declared '-- forge:backward-compatible'.

There is no "down": forge rolls forward only. A bad migration is repaired by a
new forward migration written against the state the database is actually in.

Migrations are read from db/migrations/ by default. Nothing else needs to be
installed.

The connection string can be provided via --dsn or, if the flag is omitted,
the DATABASE_URL environment variable.

Examples:
  forge db migrate up --dsn=`<dsn>`
  forge db migrate up                   # picks up $DATABASE_URL
  DATABASE_URL=... forge db migrate status
  forge db migrate version
  forge db migrate force 20240102150405

```
reliant forge db migrate [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`force`](#reliant-forge-db-migrate-force) | Clear a dirty migration state by recording a version without running SQL |
| [`status`](#reliant-forge-db-migrate-status) | Show migration status |
| [`up`](#reliant-forge-db-migrate-up) | Apply pending migrations |
| [`version`](#reliant-forge-db-migrate-version) | Show the current migration version |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge db migrate force

Clear a dirty migration state by recording a version without running SQL

Record `<version>` as the applied migration version WITHOUT running any SQL.

Reach for this when a migration failed part-way and golang-migrate marked the
state dirty. Nothing else will run until that flag clears — including
'forge db migrate up', which refuses on a dirty version — so this is the way
out of that loop.

Forcing asserts a fact; it does not verify one. Forge cannot know how much of
the failed migration actually landed, so repair the schema FIRST (inspect it
with 'forge db introspect', then finish or undo the partial migration by hand),
and force only once the database matches what that version intended. Forcing
past a migration whose SQL never ran leaves the schema permanently behind what
forge believes is applied.

The applied record (schema_migrations_applied) follows the version: the
versions a force asserts are recorded as this directory's files, and rows above
the forced version are dropped, so the next 'up' accepts the database.

Examples:
  forge db introspect                    # see what actually landed
  forge db migrate force 20240102150405  # then clear the flag
  forge db migrate up                    # and catch up

```
reliant forge db migrate force [version] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dir` | `string` | - | Migrations directory (default: forge.yaml database.migrations_dir, else db/migrations; a relative path resolves against the project root, not the CWD) |
| `--dsn` | `string` | - | Database connection string (falls back to $DATABASE_URL) |

---

#### reliant forge db migrate status

Show migration status

```
reliant forge db migrate status [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dir` | `string` | - | Migrations directory (default: forge.yaml database.migrations_dir, else db/migrations; a relative path resolves against the project root, not the CWD) |
| `--dsn` | `string` | - | Database connection string (falls back to $DATABASE_URL) |

---

#### reliant forge db migrate up

Apply pending migrations

Apply pending migrations.

Every migration runs under a lock_timeout (default 5s) and a statement_timeout
(default 30m): a migration that cannot get its lock fails fast instead of
queueing every query on the table behind it. A timed-out migration is rolled
back and tried again with a growing wait, up to --lock-attempts times (default
5), then fails the run with the schema unchanged. One migration overrides the
timeouts for itself with a header comment:

  -- forge:lock-timeout 30s
  -- forge:statement-timeout 2h

A duration is 500ms, 30s, 5m, 1h; "off" disables the timeout.

```
reliant forge db migrate up [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dir` | `string` | - | Migrations directory (default: forge.yaml database.migrations_dir, else db/migrations; a relative path resolves against the project root, not the CWD) |
| `--dsn` | `string` | - | Database connection string (falls back to $DATABASE_URL) |
| `--lock-attempts` | `int` | `0` | Attempts at a migration whose lock times out, the first included (default 5; 1 never retries) |
| `--lock-timeout` | `string` | - | How long one attempt at a migration waits for a lock, e.g. 5s, 30s, off (default 5s; a migration's own -- forge:lock-timeout wins) |
| `--statement-timeout` | `string` | - | Longest one statement may run, e.g. 30m, 2h, off (default 30m; a migration's own -- forge:statement-timeout wins) |
