### reliant forge kcl

Evaluate this project's KCL directly

Work with this project's KCL files directly.

A forge project's KCL resolves `import forge` from the forge binary, not from a
kcl.mod dependency or a copy on disk, so a plain `kcl run` on a project file
CANNOT evaluate it. That is by design: the binary is the module version. These
subcommands are how anything outside forge reads a value out of project KCL.

```
reliant forge kcl [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`eval`](#reliant-forge-kcl-eval) | Evaluate a KCL file or package directory and print a selected field |
| [`imports`](#reliant-forge-kcl-imports) | Show where each forge project this project's KCL imports resolves to |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge kcl eval

Evaluate a KCL file or package directory and print a selected field

Evaluate a .k file or every .k file in a package directory and print a field.

Evaluation runs in an isolated child rooted at the requested project. The
caller's working directory is never changed, including when forge is embedded.

This is THE way a script or a test reads a value from project KCL. A plain
`kcl run` cannot do it: since the forge KCL module is supplied by the forge
binary, `import forge` resolves only inside a forge evaluation. Nothing needs
`kcl` on PATH, and nothing needs to know where forge keeps its module.

Relative imports (`import lib.foo`) resolve from the source's own kcl.mod
package root; relative file.read paths resolve from the project root. It does not matter which directory
you run this from: the answer is the same from the project root and from a
subdirectory.

-S takes a dotted path, kcl-style. A numeric segment indexes a list
(`pools.0.name`). Repeat -S to get an object keyed by selector. Unlike
`kcl run -S`, a selected LIST comes back as one list rather than as one YAML
document per element, and a path that does not exist is an ERROR naming what
the document does have — not an empty result that a caller reads as a value.

--format json prints the document the way `kcl run --format json` does: keys in the
order the KCL declares them (never alphabetised), numbers as KCL wrote them,
four-space indent. A committed JSON file generated by kcl can be compared
byte for byte. --format yaml keeps the same order.

--format raw prints a scalar's own text: no quotes, no trailing newline. It is
what makes `$(...)` capture the value exactly, replacing the
`| tr -d "\n'\""` pipelines that a YAML scalar's conditional quoting forces
(and that silently corrupt a value legitimately containing a quote). It
refuses a list or an object rather than printing JSON a caller would
interpolate without noticing.

-D binds a top-level `option()`, as it does for any KCL evaluation.

The evaluation is READ-ONLY as far as forge is concerned: no port block is
claimed, no port store is written, no cluster is contacted, and no image is
built. It is not guaranteed PURE, for the same reason `env render` is not:
KCL evaluates `file.write` itself, so a file that generates a file writes it.

To evaluate an ENVIRONMENT into its Kubernetes objects, use
`cli forge env render <env>` instead.

Examples:
  cli forge kcl eval deploy/kcl/lib/kata_pool.k -S sbd.image_family --format raw
  cli forge kcl eval deploy/kcl/lib/kata_pool.k -S sbd.disk_size_gb --format raw
  cli forge kcl eval deploy/kcl/lib/daemon_placement.k -S daemon_placement | jq -r '.prod.context'
  cli forge kcl eval deploy/kcl/lib/platform_local.k -S cloudnative_pg.name -S cloudnative_pg.namespace
  cli forge kcl eval deploy/kcl/lib/kata_pool.k                       # the whole document

```
reliant forge kcl eval <file-or-directory> [-S <path>]... [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--format` | `string` | `json` | Output format: [json yaml raw] (raw prints a scalar's own text, unquoted and with no trailing newline) |
| `--option`, `-D` | `stringArray` | `[]` | Bind a top-level KCL option, key=value (repeatable) |
| `--select`, `-S` | `stringArray` | `[]` | Print only this dotted field path (repeatable; repeated paths yield an object keyed by path) |

---

#### reliant forge kcl imports

Show where each forge project this project's KCL imports resolves to

List the forge projects deploy/kcl/kcl.mod imports under [forge_projects],
and where each one resolves.

An imported project's KCL is read at the version this project's go.mod pins
its Go module to — a go.work use or a directory replace reads that directory
(a sibling checkout), otherwise the module cache at the required version,
verified against go.sum. There is no separate KCL pin. Resolution refuses an
imported project that pins a different forge than this one (version skew).

--json prints the resolution as a JSON array: name, module, version (empty
for a directory), via (the declaration that decided it), module_dir, dir (the
KCL package root) and forge_pin.

Examples:
  cli forge kcl imports
  cli forge kcl imports --json | jq -r '.[].dir'

```
reliant forge kcl imports [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print the resolution as JSON |

---

### reliant forge ledger

Move and inspect deploy ledgers

Work with an entire deploy ledger — the releases a project has cut and the
promotions that bound its environments.

An environment's ledger is chosen by its DECLARATION, in one place and with no
flag: an env whose KCL declares `forge.ControlPlane` records on that control
plane; every other env records in this machine's ledger under
$FORGE_LEDGER_HOME (default ~/.forge/ledger), keyed by project so every
worktree shares one history.

FORGE_LEDGER=machine overrides that for every env and every forge process that
inherits it: nothing is read from or written to a declared control plane. Use
it for hermetic tests and scripts. $FORGE_LEDGER_HOME only relocates the
machine ledger; it never keeps a declared env off its control plane.
`ledger where <env>` says which ledger an env uses, and why.

`import` is how history reaches whichever store an env selected. forge once
kept promotions in `.forge/promotions/<env>.jsonl` inside the checkout; those
files are no longer read, and a command refuses while the checkout holds
records the selected ledger has never imported.

```
reliant forge ledger [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`export`](#reliant-forge-ledger-export) | Write the selected ledger to a directory, to VERIFY an import |
| [`import`](#reliant-forge-ledger-import) | Import a ledger from a checkout's committed history, or from another ledger directory |
| [`show`](#reliant-forge-ledger-show) | Print this machine's ledger records — promotions, applies and local sessions |
| [`where`](#reliant-forge-ledger-where) | Print which backend holds an environment's ledger, and the declaration that chose it |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge ledger export

Write the selected ledger to a directory, to VERIFY an import

Write a ledger out in file-ledger format, so an import can be checked against
the files it came from.

THIS IS A VERIFICATION AID, NOT A BACKUP. It exists for one sequence:

  cli forge ledger import --from-git --rev origin/main --apply
  cli forge ledger export --dir /tmp/verify
  diff -r /tmp/verify/promotions .forge/promotions

If those agree, the import lost nothing — which is what you need to know
before the committed ledger is deleted from the repository.

It is NOT a way to keep a second copy of deploy history. A copy nothing writes
to is stale as soon as the next promotion lands, and a stale ledger that looks
authoritative is the failure the ledger was moved out of the checkout to end.
There is no git export in either direction, and no default directory — a
default is a thing people start to rely on.

The output is the ledger's own canonical JSON, so a diff is meaningful rather
than a re-rendering. Four differences against a retired git ledger are
expected and are printed with every run.

Examples:
  cli forge ledger export --dir /tmp/verify
  cli forge ledger export --dir /tmp/verify --env prod   # the ledger prod selected

```
reliant forge ledger export --dir <directory> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dir` | `string` | - | The directory to write to (required; use a throwaway path) |
| `--env` | `string` | - | Export the ledger this environment selected (default: this machine's ledger for the project) |

---

#### reliant forge ledger import

Import a ledger from a checkout's committed history, or from another ledger directory

Read a ledger from somewhere else and record it in the one each environment
selected. A DRY RUN unless you pass `--apply`.

TWO SOURCES:

  --from-git [--rev `<ref>`]        the retired in-checkout ledger
                                 (.forge/releases/*.json and
                                 .forge/promotions/`<env>`.jsonl) as COMMITTED at
                                 a rev. Default rev: origin/main.
  --from-file-ledger `<dir>`       a machine ledger directory, for an env whose
                                 KCL newly declares a control plane.

IT READS A REV, NEVER THE WORKING TREE. The retired ledger was committed, so
the repository is the authority. A `.forge/promotions` deleted but not
committed still has its history, and a half-written local edit is not history
at all.

HISTORY IS NEVER RE-JUDGED. Every record is admitted exactly as written,
including the uncomfortable parts — a release cut from a dirty tree is imported
saying so. Promotion ids and timestamps are preserved verbatim: the id is how a
re-run recognises what it already recorded, and the timestamp is the order an
environment actually moved in.

IDEMPOTENT. Running `--apply` twice records once. A run interrupted half-way
is re-run, not repaired.

IT REFUSES AN ENVIRONMENT THAT ALREADY HOLDS PROMOTIONS OF ITS OWN. An
append-only log's current binding is its last line, so interleaving imported
history with promotions made since would leave the environment reading whatever
sorted last. The remedy is to import first.

WHERE IT WRITES is each environment's selected ledger: an env whose KCL declares
`forge.ControlPlane` imports INTO THAT CONTROL PLANE. The plan labels every
target as hosted or machine, and --apply names a hosted target again on the
line before it writes. To keep an import on this machine — a test, a script, a
scratch copy of a real env — set FORGE_LEDGER=machine. Setting only
$FORGE_LEDGER_HOME does not: it relocates the machine ledger, and a declared
env still imports into its control plane.

Examples:
  cli forge ledger import --from-git                        # dry run against origin/main
  cli forge ledger import --from-git --rev origin/main --apply
  cli forge ledger import --from-file-ledger ~/.forge/ledger/myproj-ab12cd34 --apply
  FORGE_LEDGER=machine FORGE_LEDGER_HOME=$(mktemp -d) cli forge ledger import --from-git --rev HEAD --apply

```
reliant forge ledger import [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--apply` | `bool` | - | Record the import. Without it, print the plan and change nothing |
| `--from-file-ledger` | `string` | - | Read a machine ledger directory ($FORGE_LEDGER_HOME/`<project-id>`/) instead |
| `--from-git` | `bool` | - | Read the retired in-checkout ledger (.forge/releases, .forge/promotions) as committed at --rev |
| `--rev` | `string` | `origin/main` | The git rev to read the checkout's ledger at (--from-git only) |

---

#### reliant forge ledger show

Print this machine's ledger records — promotions, applies and local sessions

Read the records in this machine's ledger: each environment's promotions and
applies, and the local stacks running here.

WHY THIS EXISTS. An environment that declares `forge.ControlPlane` has hosted
rows, and a UI reads them directly. An environment with no control plane does
not: its history and its running stacks exist only in one machine's ledger
under $FORGE_LEDGER_HOME. So this is how that half becomes visible — the
reliant daemon runs it and the UI renders the result.

It is therefore a DAEMON-DEPENDENT view, and the only one. A surface showing
it must distinguish "no sessions" from "cannot see sessions": they are
different facts, and collapsing them tells a user their dev stack is down when
really their daemon is.

Local sessions are PRESENCE ONLY. A session is an observation — never a
promotion, never a deploy target — and nothing reads it for policy or billing.

Examples:
  cli forge ledger show                  # every environment
  cli forge ledger show dev --json       # one environment, for the daemon

```
reliant forge ledger show [environment] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print the records as JSON (the form the reliant daemon's hook returns) |

---

#### reliant forge ledger where

Print which backend holds an environment's ledger, and the declaration that chose it

Say where an environment's promotions and releases are recorded, and why.

Selection is DECLARATIVE and has no flag: an environment whose KCL declares
`forge.ControlPlane` records on that control plane — whatever its kind,
including local — and every other environment records in this machine's
ledger under $FORGE_LEDGER_HOME (default ~/.forge/ledger), keyed by project so
that every worktree of a project shares one history.

ONE OVERRIDE: FORGE_LEDGER=machine sends every environment to this machine's
ledger, whatever it declares, and nothing is read from or written to the
declared control plane. It is for hermetic tests and scripts that render or
import a real environment. $FORGE_LEDGER_HOME alone does NOT do this: it says
WHERE the machine ledger is, not WHETHER an environment uses it. A script can
assert its run stays local with `ledger where <env> --json` (backend
"machine", override "FORGE_LEDGER=machine").

This renders the environment and reports the declaration it FOUND. It does not
infer the answer from which store happens to hold records: a project that has
moved an environment to a control plane still has its old machine-ledger files
on disk, and "where are the records" is a different question from "where do
records go".

Examples:
  cli forge ledger where prod
  cli forge ledger where dev --json

```
reliant forge ledger where <environment> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print `{env, backend, location, because, declaration, override, project}` as JSON |

---

### reliant forge lint

Run linters on the project (report-only: writes nothing unless --fix)

Run various linters on the Forge project.

This command will:
- Run standard Go linters (golangci-lint)
- Run proto linters (buf lint)
- Lint and TYPECHECK every frontend the project declares (forge resolves the
  set from forge.yaml — no shell loop over frontends/*/ required). The
  typecheck runs the project's own "typecheck" npm script when it has one,
  and otherwise runs the frontend's TypeScript compiler with --noEmit.
  Severity is lint.frontend.typecheck (error | warn | off) in forge.yaml.
- Optionally run targeted rule sets (--contract, --db, --migration-safety,
  --conventions, --tests). Name several to run them all under one verdict:
  forge lint --conventions --contract

REPORT-ONLY BY DEFAULT. 'forge lint' is a gate, and a gate reports: it writes
NOTHING. A file a fixer would rewrite is printed as the diff the rewrite would
apply and fails the run, so the author sees exactly what to change. (--check
and --no-fix spell the same thing explicitly; --json is always detect-only.)

--fix applies the mechanical fixes, REWRITING FILES IN PLACE, then gates on the
residue: it formats your Go source (never generated files) to the project's
own gate — gofmt, plus goimports' import grouping with the local-prefixes your
golangci-lint config declares, only when that config enables goimports —
rewrites log calls to pass their context and contract constructions to
their traced NewInstrumented (tracing lint), runs each frontend's
own prettier over the src/ files you own, and runs eslint --fix on frontends.
It never rewrites a file the project's own formatter gate accepts, and it
never applies golangci-lint's fixes: staticcheck's and gocritic's rewrites
are code changes (SA9004 changes a constant's type), so they stay findings.
Every Go rewrite is verified — the packages it touched are type-checked, and a
fix that leaves one failing is taken back and reported. Under --scope it
touches only files inside the scope, and naming Go package paths (forge lint
--fix ./internal/x/...) likewise confines the fixers to those packages.

SEVERITY IS ONE DIAL: forge.yaml lint.rules maps a rule ID — the [rule] each
finding prints — to error, warn or off, and it grades every forge lane the same
way in every mode: lint.rules: `{forge-config-deps: error}` makes the advisory
config-deps lane gate; `{forgeconv-handler-file-size: warn}` lets a gating rule
be adopted gradually; "*" sets every forge rule. Another tool's linters keep
their own config (.golangci.yml, buf.yaml, eslint), and a key forge does not
grade — a typo, another tool's rule, a lane's could-not-run — is a lint error.

NEW RULES WARN FIRST: a rule that joined forge lint (or was tightened) in a
release your forge.yaml forge_version has not been through yet reports its
findings as WARNINGS — "warn: [rule] is new in v1.0.0 and gates from
v1.0.1" — and they do not fail the lane, the exit code or --gate-json. Bump
the pin to the gates-from release and they gate. lint.rules wins: `{rule: error}`
gates a new rule today, `{rule: warn}` keeps it a warning past its gates-from
release, `{rule: off}` drops it. --json carries the note as a "grace" object on
the finding. A project with no pin (or forge's own tree) gates everything.

Examples:
  forge lint                     # Report only: print the diffs, write nothing
  forge lint --fix               # Apply the mechanical fixes, then gate
  forge lint --fix ./internal/x/...  # ...only in the packages you are working on
  forge lint --skip-frontends    # Backend-only gate; no Node toolchain needed
  forge lint --contract          # Run contract interface enforcement linter
  forge lint --db                # Run DB entity lint rules
  forge lint --migration-safety  # Run SQL migration safety checks
  forge lint --conventions       # Run forge convention rules (proto, Go tree,
                                 # frontend env, hook-test rows vs their hooks)
  forge lint --generated-drift   # Fail if a forge-generated ("DO NOT EDIT")
                                 # file was hand-edited
  forge lint --tests             # Warn on handlers and generated hooks with
                                 # no test (warnings only)
  forge lint --config-deps       # Flag scalar Deps fields — a scalar is
                                 # configuration, not a collaborator; prints the
                                 # exact proto block + AppConfig line to write
  forge lint --column-markers    # Fail on a COMMENT ON TABLE/VIEW/COLUMN/
                                 # CONSTRAINT carrying a forge:* marker that
                                 # object does not read
  forge lint --fixture-drift     # Execute every literal fixture statement in
                                 # a scaffolded handlers_crud_test.go against
                                 # the CURRENT schema (shadow postgres,
                                 # rolled back) and report postgres's own
                                 # error per statement it rejects
  forge lint --time-bucketing    # Flag a two-argument date_trunc over a
                                 # TIMESTAMPTZ column — it truncates in the
                                 # SESSION timezone, so every bucketed total
                                 # moves with the deploy host. Pin the zone:
                                 # date_trunc('day', col, 'UTC')
  forge lint --proto-markers     # Flag a .proto comment carrying an
                                 # unrecognized forge:* marker (a misspelled
                                 # one does nothing and warns nowhere)
  forge lint --create-nullability # Fail when a field's optional label
                                 # disagrees between an entity message and
                                 # its Create`<Entity>`Request (the flattened
                                 # request silently drops write presence)
  forge lint --computed-fields   # FAIL on a forge:computed field that no
                                 # non-generated Go file assigns — nothing
                                 # populates it, so the column default ships
  forge lint --read-only-fields  # FAIL on a forge:read-only column that
                                 # nothing populates — no write path, no
                                 # meaningful DEFAULT, so every row ships as
                                 # the zero with no other symptom at all.
                                 # Both WARN instead ("pending: implement X")
                                 # while the service still holds forge's own
                                 # unwired rpc stubs
  forge lint --secrets           # FAIL on a platform-secrets defect, with
                                 # file:line and its escape
  forge lint --guarded-fields    # Flag a scaffolded edit page whose
                                 # update_mask still writes a column a
                                 # custom rpc guards (forge:guards) — the
                                 # form bypasses the rpc's own rules, and
                                 # scaffold-once means regenerating cannot
                                 # fix it
  forge lint --static-export     # For a Next.js frontend that must be a static
                                 # export (output: static, or bound to a static
                                 # runtime): FAIL on what the export refuses
                                 # ([id] without generateStaticParams, server
                                 # actions, cookies(), next/image, no export)
                                 # and WARN on what it drops (middleware,
                                 # rewrites, POST handlers, no trailingSlash
                                 # on a bucket). CI's next build is the
                                 # authority; this says it first, file:line
  forge lint --tracing           # Report where a request's trace is cut (a
                                 # log call that drops the ctx in scope, a
                                 # raw *http.Client or sql.Open, a contract
                                 # built without its decorator) and rewrite
                                 # each log call to its ...Context form and
                                 # each such New to NewInstrumented
  forge lint --proto-options     # Flag a (forge.v1.*) annotation naming an
                                 # option field forge's descriptors do not
                                 # define — it compiles, and forge reads it
                                 # nowhere
  forge lint --vendored-protos   # Fail when proto/forge/v1/forge.proto has
                                 # drifted from this forge binary's copy
  forge lint --config-reach      # Flag config fields no binary or frontend
                                 # loads
  forge lint --optional-deps-guard  # Flag unguarded derefs of an optional dep
  forge lint --frontend-stores   # Flag Zustand stores holding server data that
                                 # belongs in a generated React Query hook
  forge lint --json              # Machine-readable findings for sub-agents / CI
                                 # (schema in lint_json.go; exit code matches
                                 # text mode; combines with the targeted flags
                                 # above, but not with --fix / --suggest-*)
  forge lint --quiet             # Only what FAILED, then the verdict as the
                                 # last line — safe to pipe through head/tail.
                                 # Combines with any targeted flag above:
                                 #   forge lint --read-only-fields --quiet
  forge lint --show-suppressed   # Every suppression the project declares, with
                                 # file:line and reason. Lints nothing
  forge lint --scope internal/handlers/jobs --scope proto/services/jobs
                                 # Only findings in files under those paths.
                                 # golangci-lint/contract run on the scope's
                                 # packages; whole-project linters (frontend
                                 # lint, graph, component-drift, config-reach) are
                                 # skipped and named in the verdict, so run an
                                 # unscoped 'forge lint' before merging

The advisory rules above are warnings by default — they fail the build only
when lint.rules sets them to error.

Additional maintainer/debug flags exist (forge-repo internals, wiring
audits, suggest-* helpers); run 'forge lint --help-dev' to list them.

```
reliant forge lint [paths...] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-skip` | `stringArray` | `[]` | Accept that a gating lane may NOT run here, by name and with a reason: LANE[,LANE...]=REASON (repeatable), e.g. --allow-skip 'frontend-typecheck=frontends typecheck in the web job'. A gating lane that could not run (a tool missing, a frontend with no node_modules) fails forge lint otherwise; an accepted one is listed as SKIPPED (allowed) in the verdict. forge.yaml lint.allow_skip is the project-level form. Accepts only could-not-run: a lane that ran and found problems still fails |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--check` | `bool` | - | Report only: write nothing, print the diff each fix would apply, and exit non-zero if any is pending. The default; beats --fix (also spelled --no-fix) |
| `--closed-sets` | `bool` | - | Fail when a CHECK declared forge:closed-set `<set>` does not list exactly that Go set, and print the migration that fixes it |
| `--column-markers` | `bool` | - | Fail on a COMMENT ON TABLE/VIEW/COLUMN/CONSTRAINT carrying a forge: marker that object does not read |
| `--computed-fields` | `bool` | - | Flag a forge:computed field that no non-generated Go file assigns — nothing populates it, so the insert takes the column default. FAILS the build, except while the service still holds forge-scaffolded unwired rpc stubs (then a warning naming them) |
| `--config-deps` | `bool` | - | Flag scalar Deps fields — scalars are configuration; declare a `<Component>`Config block in proto/`<project>`/config/v1 and take it as a typed field (warnings by default, lint.rules forge-config-deps: error gates them) |
| `--config-reach` | `bool` | - | Flag config fields that no binary and no frontend loads — with per-binary configs, an unbound config message generates but is never loaded (warnings only) |
| `--contract` | `bool` | - | Run contract interface enforcement linter |
| `--conventions` | `bool` | - | Run forge convention rules (proto, Go tree, frontend env, and hook-test rows that contradict their regenerated hook) |
| `--create-nullability` | `bool` | - | Fail when a field's optional label disagrees between an entity message and its Create`<Entity>`Request — the flattened request drops write presence silently |
| `--db-grants` | `bool` | - | Fail on a credential-shaped column (token, password, key, ciphertext, hash…) that carries neither forge:secret nor forge:readable `<reason>`: the read-only forge_ro role is refused it until someone decides |
| `--fix` | `bool` | - | Apply the mechanical fixes in place (Go formatting, the tracing log rewrite, frontend prettier, eslint --fix), each Go rewrite type-checked and taken back if it breaks its package. Never golangci-lint fixes. Off by default: lint is a gate and reports |
| `--fixture-drift` | `bool` | - | Execute every literal fixture statement in a scaffolded handlers_crud_test.go against the current schema — migrations applied to a shadow postgres, each statement run in a rolled-back transaction — and report postgres's own error for each one it rejects: a GENERATED column, a UNIQUE collision, a dangling foreign key, a CHECK a later migration added (warnings only) |
| `--frontend-stores` | `bool` | - | Flag Zustand stores that import generated Connect clients (warnings only) |
| `--gate-json` | `string` | - | Also write this run's result to `FILE` as a gate document, for `forge gate record` or `forge env deploy --gate`. A FILE, not a stdout mode: the findings and the exit code are unchanged. |
| `--generated-drift` | `bool` | - | Fail when a forge-generated ("Code generated by forge. DO NOT EDIT.") file was edited after forge wrote it |
| `--go-markers` | `bool` | - | Flag //forge: comments in .go files that forge does not read — an unknown marker name, or a known marker in a spelling its parser ignores (“//forge:exclude-contract — why” instead of “: why”); each is an error naming file:line and the expected form |
| `--graph` | `bool` | - | Check the composition graph (internal/app) as a whole: every component and provider is reached by some root, and every listener serves only services of its chain's audience. FAILS the build on an error (graph mode only) |
| `--guarded-fields` | `bool` | - | Flag a scaffolded edit page whose update_mask still names a column declared 'forge:guards' — saving the form writes it raw and bypasses the rpc that owns it, and pages are scaffold-once so 'forge generate' cannot repair them (warnings only) |
| `--help-dev` | `bool` | - | List maintainer/debug flags hidden from --help |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Output findings as JSON (see lint_json.go header for the schema; exit code matches text mode) |
| `--migration-safety` | `bool` | - | Run SQL migration safety checks |
| `--no-fix` | `bool` | - | Same as --check: write nothing, print the diff each fix would apply, and fail if any is pending |
| `--optional-deps-guard` | `bool` | - | Flag unguarded derefs of // forge:optional-dep Deps fields (warnings by default, lint.rules forge-optional-deps-guard: error gates them; suppress a confirmed-safe site with // forge:optional-checked on the deref line) |
| `--proto-markers` | `bool` | - | Flag .proto comments containing forge: that match no known proto marker — a misspelled marker is inert and warns nowhere (warnings only) |
| `--proto-options` | `bool` | - | Flag (forge.v1.*) annotation fields this forge binary's descriptors do not define — a retired or misspelled option field compiles under buf and is read by nothing (warnings only) |
| `--quiet`, `-q` | `bool` | - | Print only what failed: a one-line summary per failing linter, its error findings, and the verdict as the LAST line (a clean run is one line). With --fix, fixes are applied silently; combines with the targeted flags and --scope |
| `--read-only-fields` | `bool` | - | Flag a forge:read-only field whose column nothing populates — no non-generated Go file assigns it, no meaningful DEFAULT, not GENERATED — so every row ships as the type's zero with no error anywhere. FAILS the build, except while the service still holds forge-scaffolded unwired rpc stubs (then a warning naming them) |
| `--scope` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Report only findings in files under `PATH` (repeatable or comma-separated, project-relative). Go linters run on PATH's packages only; file-anchored linters report only findings under PATH; whole-project linters (frontend lint, graph, component-drift, config-reach) cannot be scoped, are skipped, and are named in the verdict. With --fix, only files under PATH are rewritten |
| `--secrets` | `bool` | - | Judge the platform secrets model per env, with file:line: no ClusterSecretStore/ClusterExternalSecret/PushSecret, ExternalSecret policy (Orphan or Merge, Retain, namespaced store, explicit keys), every Secret a workload reads is produced by exactly one declaration that lists the key, an immutable store name is pinned at 1, an out_of_band_reason that explains, no reasonless OpenBao wildcard read, and (warning) an OpenBao escrowed inside its own cluster. FAILS the build on an error; every message names its escape |
| `--show-suppressed` | `bool` | - | List every suppression the project declares, with file:line, what it covers and the stated reason: forge.yaml lint.rules (off/warn) and contracts.exclude, //forge:exclude-contract markers, forge:lint-disable* and nolint directives, and the migration and optional-dep pragmas. Read-only: runs no linter and exits 0; combines with --json and --scope |
| `--skip-frontends` | `bool` | - | Skip the whole frontend lane (eslint/stylelint + TypeScript typecheck + static-export) for a backend-only gate that needs no Node toolchain |
| `--slow-tests` | `bool` | - | Fail on a Go test that does slow work without being marked slow — execs go/forge/kcl/node/npm/git/docker/…, drives containers, or dials an external host, with no testkit.Slow(t, why) or testing.Short() guard on its path — so it runs in the blocking -short tier, and a file its child process reads is invisible to the test cache |
| `--static-export` | `bool` | - | For each Next.js frontend that must build to a static export (forge.yaml output: static, or bound to forge.OnHosted / OnBucket / OnFirebase in any env), report with file:line what the export cannot serve. FAILS on what next build refuses (a dynamic route without generateStaticParams, server actions, next/headers, next/image without images.unoptimized, a build that is not an export); WARNS on what it silently drops (middleware, non-GET route handlers, ungated rewrites/redirects/headers, a bucket-bound export without trailingSlash) |
| `--strict` | `bool` | - | Escalate advisory findings to errors so they fail the build / CI: RPCs missing a (forge.v1.method) auth-posture annotation, contract refusals in a tree with no forge.yaml, and an advisory lane's own could-not-run report (the typed-config guardrail when golangci-lint never reported). Not needed for a gating lane that could NOT run: that always fails, unless --allow-skip accepts it |
| `--tests` | `bool` | - | Run test-presence rules (handler-tests-use-tdd + frontend-hook-tests; warnings only — the gating hook-test-shape rule runs in --conventions) |
| `--time-bucketing` | `bool` | - | Flag a two-argument date_trunc over a TIMESTAMPTZ column — postgres truncates it in the SESSION timezone, which the driver sets from the client host, so every bucketed total is attributed to the wrong day by an amount that changes with the deploy host. Pin the zone: date_trunc('day', col, 'UTC') (warnings only) |
| `--tracing` | `bool` | - | Report where a request's trace is cut: a log call made with a context in scope but not passed it (no trace_id), an *http.Client or database pool opened without forge's traced constructors, a contract package constructed without its generated decorator. With --fix, rewrites every log call to its ...Context form and every undecorated contract construction to NewInstrumented; forge.yaml lint.tracing.log_wrappers adds the project's own logging helpers |
| `--vendored-protos` | `bool` | - | Fail when a vendored proto (proto/forge/v1/forge.proto) differs from the copy embedded in this forge binary — forge's upgrade path does not track these copies, so drift is otherwise invisible |

---

### reliant forge login

Authenticate to the control plane(s) this project declares

Obtain a credential for a hosted control plane and store it in the shared
credentials file (~/.config/forge/credentials.json, 0600), keyed by the endpoint.

Login is about WHO you are, not WHERE you deploy, so it takes no environment.
With no flag it logs into EVERY distinct control plane this project's envs
declare in KCL:

    control_plane = forge.ControlPlane `{}`    # Reliant cloud (https://admin.reliantapi.com)
    control_plane = forge.ControlPlane \{     # any other control plane
        endpoint = "http://127.0.0.1:8090"
    \}

(two envs declaring the same endpoint share one login). When nothing declares
one — outside a project, or before any env does — it logs into Reliant cloud.
--endpoint names one directly. There is no "current" server: every forge
command finds its credential by the endpoint its own env declares, so which
env a command acts on is always the env it names — never a login's side
effect.

INTERACTIVE (a human): the OAuth authorization-code flow with PKCE. forge
opens your browser at `<endpoint>`/oauth/authorize, you sign in and approve,
and the browser returns a one-time code to a temporary listener on a
loopback port (chosen by the OS, so two logins can run at once). forge
redeems it at `<endpoint>`/oauth/token for an access token (rlat_…, 90 days).

NON-INTERACTIVE (CI): pass --token (with --endpoint when the project declares
more than one control plane), or set the environment variable the environment
declares (FORGE_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN by default) and skip login entirely. A
pipeline has no browser, which is what org tokens are for.

SIGNED IN TO RELIANT? You do not need this command. `reliant forge …` and every
shell a Reliant agent runs set $FORGE_CREDENTIAL_HELPER, and forge asks that
helper for a short-lived token minted from your Reliant session for exactly the
control plane an env declares. forge login is for standalone forge (no Reliant)
and for overriding the session with a different identity.

CREDENTIAL PRECEDENCE, when any forge command talks to the control plane:

    1. --token           explicit, beats everything
    2. $`<token_env>`      the env var the environment declares — CI
    3. the credentials file entry for that env's endpoint — what this command stores
    4. $FORGE_CREDENTIAL_HELPER — a host application's session (Reliant's);
       also used when the stored login (3) has expired

```
reliant forge login [--endpoint URL] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--endpoint` | `string` | - | Log into this control plane URL only, instead of every one the project declares (default when none is declared: Reliant cloud) |
| `--no-verify` | `bool` | - | With --token: store it without checking it against the endpoint |
| `--token` | `string` | - | Store this token instead of running the browser flow |

---

```
reliant forge logout [--endpoint URL] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--endpoint` | `string` | - | Forget this control plane URL only, instead of every one the project declares |

### reliant forge mcp

Run forge as an MCP server on stdio, so any agent harness can drive it

Run forge as a Model Context Protocol server on stdio.

Any MCP-capable harness (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, ...) can then call forge's
introspection, skill and lint/generate verbs as tools, with no memory file in
the project telling it how. The server says what forge is, and tells the model to
load the 'forge' skill first.

Tools (each takes an optional "dir": the project to act on; default is the
directory the server was started in, or -C):

  read-only   capabilities   every verb, lint analyzer and marker
              annotations    the entity-authoring vocabulary
              libraries      forge/pkg and web-runtime APIs
              audit          what THIS project currently is
              shapes         where every rpc/message/table/handler is, with file:line
              skill_list     the skill catalog
              skill_search   find a skill by keyword
              skill_load     print one skill
  mutating    lint           forge lint --quiet [--scope PATH...]
              generate       forge generate [--dry-run | --check]

Each tool runs the same command in-process; it does not shell out to a forge
binary, and it does not hand a project pinned to another forge over to that
forge (it reports the skew instead).

Register it:

  Claude Code   claude mcp add forge -- forge mcp
  Codex         codex mcp add forge -- forge mcp
  Cursor        \{"mcpServers": \{"forge": `{"command": "forge", "args": ["mcp"]}`\}\}
                in .cursor/mcp.json

docs/mcp.md has the config files in full.

```
reliant forge mcp
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |

| [`alerts`](#reliant-forge-obs-alerts) | Alert rules declared for an environment |
| [`changes`](#reliant-forge-obs-changes) | What changed? Promotions, rollbacks, failed deploys and activity, newest first |
| [`check`](#reliant-forge-obs-check) | Did the deploy work? Judge a release by the window after it against the window before |
| [`dashboards`](#reliant-forge-obs-dashboards) | Work with the dashboards a release carries |
| [`endpoints`](#reliant-forge-obs-endpoints) | The slowest or most failing endpoints |
| [`issue`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue) | Everything needed to fix one issue; resolve, ignore and unresolve triage it |
| [`issues`](#reliant-forge-obs-issues) | Grouped errors: what is failing, how often, since which release |
| [`logs`](#reliant-forge-obs-logs) | Search and tail logs |
| [`metrics`](#reliant-forge-obs-metrics) | List and query metrics; print the declared schema |
| [`profile`](#reliant-forge-obs-profile) | Export an aggregated continuous profile as pprof, speedscope or collapsed stacks |
| [`query`](#reliant-forge-obs-query) | Anything else: raw SQL or a dashboard panel, with the scope bound |
| [`session`](#reliant-forge-obs-session) | What one user did: a text timeline of navigations, clicks, failed requests and errors |
| [`sessions`](#reliant-forge-obs-sessions) | Real-user browser sessions: who hit errors, and which have a replay |
| [`status`](#reliant-forge-obs-status) | What is wrong right now? Services, open issues, alerts, telemetry and the release in force |
| [`trace`](#reliant-forge-obs-trace) | One trace as a tree, with its errors and logs |
| [`traces`](#reliant-forge-obs-traces) | Find traces: the slowest, the failing, by route |
| [`workloads`](#reliant-forge-obs-workloads) | Restarts, crash loops, last termination reasons and failing jobs, per workload |

**Flags:**

Alert rules declared for an environment


**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |

| [`run`](#reliant-forge-obs-alerts-run) | Evaluate the env's alert rules continuously and deliver what fires through the local Alertmanager |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |

#### reliant forge obs alerts check

Evaluate the env's alert rules against the local ClickStack and print what would fire

Evaluate the env's resolved alert rules (your forge.AlertRule entries plus the
generated defaults you did not disable, exactly what `forge env shape` records)
against the local ClickStack's ClickHouse, once, and print every instance that
is returning a row now: its labels and its value.

Nothing is notified and nothing is stored. A rule's `for` hold time is NOT applied:
this answers "what does the condition return right now", so a rule listed here
fires in production only once it has kept returning for its `for`.

Each expr runs read-only with a timeout and a row cap (--timeout, --max-rows);
a rule that errors is reported and does not stop the others.

  cli forge obs alerts check               # the dev env
  cli forge obs alerts check --env staging
  cli forge obs alerts check --rule ServiceErrorRateHigh --sql

Needs the local ClickStack running (`forge env up`).

```
reliant forge obs alerts check [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | `dev` | environment whose rules to evaluate |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print the result as JSON |
| `--max-rows` | `int` | `200` | a rule returning more rows than this is reported as an error |
| `--rule` | `string` | - | evaluate only this alert name |
| `--sql` | `bool` | - | print each rule's expr instead of running it |
| `--timeout` | `duration` | `20s` | per-rule query timeout |

---

#### reliant forge obs alerts run

Evaluate the env's alert rules continuously and deliver what fires through the local Alertmanager

The local alerting loop's evaluator, which `forge env up` runs as the `alerts` host
workload (deploy/kcl/dev/main.k). It

  * evaluates the rules `forge env up` rendered into .forge/observability/alert-rules.json
    (the env's forge.AlertRule entries plus the generated defaults, exactly what
    `forge env shape` records) on their intervals, `for` applied, against the
    local ClickStack;
  * posts every firing and resolved alert to the local Alertmanager
    (--alertmanager), labelled reliant_org_id="local" and reliant_env_id=`<env>`,
    which routes it with the control plane's own configuration renderer;
  * receives the in-app receiver's deliveries on --listen and prints one line
    per alert, so .forge/logs/`<env>`/alerts.log is the local in-app inbox.

The rules file is re-read when it changes, so a re-run of `forge env up` updates
the rules without a restart.

  cli forge obs alerts run --env dev --alertmanager http://127.0.0.1:9093 --listen 0.0.0.0:9095

```
reliant forge obs alerts run [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--alertmanager` | `string` | - | the local Alertmanager's base URL (required) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | `dev` | environment whose rules to evaluate |
| `--listen` | `string` | - | address of the in-app sink the local Alertmanager delivers to, host:port (required). It must be reachable from the Alertmanager container, so not 127.0.0.1 |
| `--max-rows` | `int` | `200` | a rule returning more rows than this fails its evaluation |
| `--timeout` | `duration` | `20s` | per-rule query timeout |

#### reliant forge obs changes

What changed? Promotions, rollbacks, failed deploys and activity, newest first

List what changed in [env] (default dev) over --since (default 2h): releases
promoted or rolled back, deploys that failed, and the activity feed (restarts,
secret rotations, triage). Locally it is this machine's ledger: promotions,
failed applies and the times the dev stack started.

  cli forge obs changes prod --since 6h
  cli forge obs changes dev --json

JSON schema forge.obs.changes/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  changes
  changes.actor
  changes.at
  changes.direction
  changes.kind
  changes.promotion_id
  changes.summary
  changes.target
  changes.verb
  changes.version
  changes.via
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.env_id
  scope.from
  scope.to
  suggested
  warnings
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs changes [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `50` | most items to return |
| `--since` | `duration` | `2h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

#### reliant forge obs check

Did the deploy work? Judge a release by the window after it against the window before

Judge one promotion of [env] (default dev): compare the [P, P+window) after it
with the [P-window, P) before it, per service, over weighted server spans, and
list what the release opened: new and regressed issues, alerts still firing,
workload restarts. The verdict is PASS, FAIL or INCONCLUSIVE. Each window
stops at the neighbouring promotion; the after window also stops at now.

  cli forge obs check prod --wait --json
  cli forge obs check prod --release v1.4.2 --window 30m
  cli forge obs check prod --fail-on error_rate,new_issue --threshold min_requests=20
  cli forge obs check prod --wait --gate-json check.json && cli forge gate record prod --from check.json
  cli forge obs check dev --since 20m          # local: no promotions, P is --since

Exit status:
  0  PASS (also INCONCLUSIVE with --allow-inconclusive)
  1  FAIL: we looked, and the release made things worse
  2  INCONCLUSIVE, or we could not look (control plane unreachable, auth
     refused, telemetry off). Both mean "not proven good"; a gate must not
     read either as a pass.
  8  --wait ran out before the after window finished

Thresholds come from the env's release_health declaration (KCL); --threshold
name=value overrides one for this call (max_error_rate_increase, max_p95_ratio, min_requests, new_issue_min_events).
The document says where each effective threshold came from: release_shape,
default or request.

JSON schema forge.obs.check/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  findings
  findings.code
  findings.message
  findings.policy
  findings.ref
  findings.service
  findings.threshold
  findings.value
  firing_alerts
  new_issues
  not_evaluated
  query_mode
  reason
  regressed_issues
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.env_id
  scope.from
  scope.to
  services
  services.after
  services.before
  services.service
  services.version_share
  standing_critical_alerts
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  subject
  subject.git_commit
  subject.previous_release
  subject.promoted_at
  subject.promotion_id
  subject.release
  suggested
  thresholds
  thresholds.name
  thresholds.source
  thresholds.value
  verdict
  waited
  windows
  windows.after
  windows.after.from
  windows.after.to
  windows.after_complete
  windows.before
  windows.before.from
  windows.before.to
  workload_findings
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs check [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-inconclusive` | `bool` | - | exit 0 on INCONCLUSIVE instead of 2 |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | local env only: print the compiled statements instead of running them |
| `--fail-on` | `string` | - | comma-separated finding codes that fail the release (default: the env's release_health.fail_on): error_rate, latency, new_issue, regressed_issue, alert_firing, workload_restarts |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--gate-json` | `string` | - | also write the verdict to `FILE` as a gate document, for `cli forge gate record --from FILE` |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--promotion` | `string` | - | judge this promotion id |
| `--release` | `string` | - | judge the promotion of this release version (default: the env's current one) |
| `--since` | `string` | - | local env only: when the release started, as a duration ago (20m) or an RFC 3339 time (default: the start of the last env up) |
| `--threshold` | `stringArray` | `[]` | override one threshold for this call, name=value (repeatable) |
| `--timeout` | `duration` | `30m0s` | with --wait: give up (exit 8) after this long |
| `--wait` | `bool` | - | poll until the after window has finished |
| `--window` | `duration` | `0s` | width of each window (default: the env's release_health.window, 15m; max 6h) |

#### reliant forge obs dashboards add-panel

Append a panel to a dashboard you own, creating it when missing

Add one panel to deploy/observability/dashboards/`<slug>`.json, the dashboard a
release ships for the Reliant console. The file is created when it does not
exist (id is the slug; title is --title, or the slug).

--panel is a file, or "-" for stdin, holding ONE panel object in the dashboard
vocabulary (for example the document 'forge obs metrics query --panel-out'
writes). It is validated exactly as 'forge obs dashboards check' validates it,
and an error carries the panel file's own line and column.

The panel's id comes from --id, else the panel's own "id", else is made from its
title and made unique within the dashboard. An id the dashboard already holds is
an error unless --replace, which swaps that panel in place. Nothing is written
when anything fails, and a repeated run with the same inputs leaves the file
byte-identical.

A slug equal to a generated dashboard's name (shop-overview for
shop-overview_gen.json) replaces that dashboard in releases; a warning says so.

```
reliant forge obs dashboards add-panel <slug> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--id` | `string` | - | id for the panel, overriding the panel's own |
| `--panel` | `string` | - | file holding one panel object, or "-" for stdin (required) |
| `--replace` | `bool` | - | replace the panel that has the same id instead of failing |
| `--title` | `string` | - | title of a dashboard being created; default: the slug |

---

#### reliant forge obs dashboards check

Validate the dashboards a release bundle would carry

Validate every dashboard the next release bundle would carry, exactly as a release
cut does, and print what would ship.

Generated dashboards come from the spec in deploy/observability/spec/ (and the
Custom metrics dashboard, when bin/observability/metrics-schema.json exists). A file
you own in deploy/observability/dashboards/ is a renderer-neutral dashboard object
(the shape of the spec's dashboards, with a "panels" array); one named like a
generated dashboard (shop-overview.json for shop-overview_gen.json) replaces it. A
HyperDX-format file (with "tiles") is for the local HyperDX only and is not shipped.

Errors carry the file, line and column of the offending value.

--against `<env>` also checks every metrics panel against that env's telemetry over
--since (default 24h): a series whose metric is stored as a different kind than
its metricType fails the check (the panel reads the wrong table, or refuses its
aggregation), and a series whose metric has no data at all is reported. No data
is often a misspelt name or one the collector drops before storage, and sometimes
a counter that only exists once something fails; --strict fails on it too.

  cli forge obs dashboards check --against prod
  cli forge obs dashboards check --against dev --since 6h --strict

```
reliant forge obs dashboards check [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--against` | `string` | - | also check every metrics panel against this env's telemetry |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | with --against: local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--output`, `-o` | `string` | `bin` | build output directory holding observability/metrics-schema.json |
| `--since` | `duration` | `24h0m0s` | with --against: the window a metric must have data in |
| `--strict` | `bool` | - | with --against: a metric with no data in the window fails the check too |

#### reliant forge obs endpoints

The slowest or most failing endpoints

Rank the env's server endpoints (server spans by service and span name) by
traffic, p95 latency, error ratio or rate: request count, errors, p50/p95/p99.
[env] defaults to dev. Counts are weighted for tail sampling.

  cli forge obs endpoints dev --sort p95 --top 5
  cli forge obs endpoints dev --sort errors --service api --since 6h

The table is the busiest 200 endpoints; --sort re-ranks within them.

JSON schema forge.obs.endpoints/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  endpoints
  endpoints.error_ratio
  endpoints.errors
  endpoints.name
  endpoints.p50_ms
  endpoints.p95_ms
  endpoints.p99_ms
  endpoints.rate_per_second
  endpoints.requests
  endpoints.service
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  sort
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs endpoints [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `1h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--sort` | `string` | `requests` | requests | p95 | errors | rate |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |
| `--top` | `int` | `10` | most endpoints to return |

#### reliant forge obs issue

Everything needed to fix one issue; resolve, ignore and unresolve triage it

Show one error issue: the issue, its latest event and call site, its events per
version, the issues that fire in the same traces, the release it first appeared
in, and the suspect commits: the commits of that release's range (or, with no
range known, the last 20) that touched the files the error was raised from,
read from this checkout with git. A hosted env also shows who it is assigned to,
the incident it belongs to (the other issues with the same cause, first seen
close together: related_count and incident_key), and the newest
--timeline-limit timeline entries, oldest first. [env] defaults to dev.

  cli forge obs issue ISS-7f3a prod
  cli forge obs issue sfp1-0123456789abcdef dev --events 10
  cli forge obs issue resolve ISS-7f3a prod --in next
  cli forge obs issue ignore ISS-7f3a prod --until 72h
  cli forge obs issue unresolve ISS-7f3a prod
  cli forge obs issue comment ISS-7f3a "checked the pool; it is the new retry loop" prod
  cli forge obs issue assign ISS-7f3a \<user-id|none> prod

Triage needs a hosted env: a local issue is a query over logs and has no state.

```
reliant forge obs issue <id> [env] [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`assign`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue-assign) | Assign an issue to a person, or clear its assignee |
| [`comment`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue-comment) | Add a comment to an issue |
| [`ignore`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue-ignore) | Silence an issue, until a time or until unignored |
| [`resolve`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue-resolve) | Mark an issue resolved, now or in a release |
| [`unresolve`](#reliant-forge-obs-issue-unresolve) | Reopen a resolved or ignored issue |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--events` | `int` | `5` | newest events to include (0 = none) |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `72h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--timeline-limit` | `int` | `10` | newest timeline entries (comments, status changes, assignment, linked chats) to include, oldest first (0 = none; at most 100) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

---

#### reliant forge obs issue assign

Assign an issue to a person, or clear its assignee

Set the issue's assignee to a user id, or clear it with "none". The user must be
a member of the issue's organization. A hosted env only: a local issue carries
no state, so this exits 2.

  cli forge obs issue assign ISS-7f3a 7c1e2a90-0b1d-4a61-9e7d-5a1d3c9f2b10 prod
  cli forge obs issue assign ISS-7f3a none prod

Use "me" for the person the verified credential acts for (including a user-owned
CLI login or helper token). The server resolves it; an organization automation
credential acts for nobody and is refused FailedPrecondition (exit 2).
  cli forge obs issue assign ISS-7f3a me prod

This requires server-side AssignIssue "me" resolution; on a control plane that
only accepts literal user ids (including CP #1402 alone), pass a user id instead.

The write sends the row version it read, so a change made since is refused
("changed since you read it", exit 1) instead of overwriting it. It is never
retried: read the issue again, then decide.

#### reliant forge obs issue comment

Add a comment to an issue

Add a comment to an issue's timeline. The text is plain text or markdown, stored
verbatim (at most 10000 characters); it is shown to everyone who can see the
issue. A hosted env only: a local issue has no discussion, so this exits 2.

  cli forge obs issue comment ISS-7f3a "reproduced on v1.4.0; the pool is exhausted" prod

The document carries the stored comment (comment.id, comment.created_at). A
credential that acts for no person cannot comment.

#### reliant forge obs issues

Grouped errors: what is failing, how often, since which release

List the env's error issues: ERROR logs grouped by fingerprint (sfp1), with how
often each fired, how many traces it touched and when. [env] defaults to dev.

--source `<name>` lists the issues of an issue source instead: records of any
level that carry issue.source=`<name>` (observe.IssueSource), such as the reports
agents file (--source llm_bug_report). They are grouped the same way, never
count as errors, and are listed only when asked for.

On a hosted env the control plane keeps the issues, so they have a status
(new, unresolved, resolved, regressed, ignored) that survives log retention, and
--since-release / --since-deploy ask "what did this release open". On a local
env they are grouped at query time over the local ClickStack: no state, every
issue is unresolved, and the counts are those of the range.

  cli forge obs issues prod                          # the open ones, last 24h
  cli forge obs issues prod --status regressed
  cli forge obs issues prod --since-release v1.4.0   # first seen in that release
  cli forge obs issues dev --service api --sort events
  cli forge obs issues prod --source llm_bug_report  # agent-filed bug reports
  cli forge obs issues prod --expand-incidents       # every issue of an incident, not the first

An INCIDENT is one cause breaking many callers: a dead database fails every
service that touches it, and each logs from its own call site, so one outage is
dozens of issues. Issues that share a cause (the SQLSTATE or refused connection
target their error carries, else its normalized error class) and first fired
within --incident-window of one another, in any service, share an incident_key.
The list collapses them under the first, which shows "+N related"; nothing is
merged and each keeps its own counts and triage state. forge obs issue `<id>`
lists the rest of its incident.
  cli forge obs issues prod --assignee me            # credential's acting person
  cli forge obs issues prod --assignee none          # unassigned
  cli forge obs issues prod --assignee 7c1e2a90-0b1d-4a61-9e7d-5a1d3c9f2b10

The server resolves "me" from the verified credential. An organization automation
token acts for nobody and is refused FailedPrecondition (exit 2); name a user id.

JSON schema forge.obs.issues/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  collapsed
  issues
  issues.affected
  issues.affected_unit
  issues.assignee
  issues.assignee.id
  issues.assignee.name
  issues.culprit
  issues.events
  issues.exception_type
  issues.first_seen
  issues.first_version
  issues.id
  issues.ignored_until
  issues.incident_key
  issues.last_seen
  issues.last_version
  issues.latest_trace_id
  issues.platform
  issues.regressed_at
  issues.related_count
  issues.resolved_at
  issues.resolved_version
  issues.service
  issues.source
  issues.status
  issues.title
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.env_id
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  sort
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs issues [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--assignee` | `string` | - | only issues assigned to this user id, me (credential's acting user), or none (unassigned) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--expand-incidents` | `bool` | - | list every issue of an incident instead of collapsing each incident under its first issue |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--incident-window` | `duration` | `10m0s` | issues of one cause first seen within this long of each other are one incident (max 1h0m0s) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `25` | most items to return |
| `--page-token` | `string` | - | resume from a next_page_token |
| `--platform` | `string` | - | server | browser |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `24h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--since-deploy` | `string` | - | only issues first seen at or after this promotion id (hosted env) |
| `--since-release` | `string` | - | only issues first seen in this release version (hosted env) |
| `--sort` | `string` | `last` | last | first | events |
| `--source` | `string` | - | list the issues of this issue source (issue.source) instead of errors, e.g. llm_bug_report |
| `--status` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only issues in this state: new, unresolved, resolved, regressed, ignored (repeatable; default the open ones) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

#### reliant forge obs logs

Search and tail logs

Search the env's logs. [env] defaults to dev. The optional query is the log query
language (the same one the control plane serves):

  service:api severity:error "connection refused"     words AND together
  body:timeout OR body:deadline                        OR / NOT / -term / (groups)
  trace_id:0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c            one trace
  attr.http.route:/v1/jobs  res.k8s.pod.name:api-0     log / resource attributes

  cli forge obs logs dev 'service:api' --severity error --since 1h
  cli forge obs logs dev --trace 0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c
  cli forge obs logs dev 'body:panic' --follow          # tail, polling every --interval (2s)
  cli forge obs logs dev 'service:api' --explain        # the compiled SQL

Newest first; --oldest reverses. --follow polls forward from now and, with
--json, writes NDJSON: one log record per line, ending with an envelope line.

JSON schema forge.obs.logs/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  logs
  logs.attributes.*
  logs.body
  logs.service
  logs.severity
  logs.span_id
  logs.time
  logs.trace_id
  query
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

#### reliant forge obs metrics declared

Print the metrics a service declares: name, type, unit, attributes, stability

Print the declared metrics schema (forge.metrics/v1) of a service.

Every metric a forge service emits is declared in a typed registry, so the full
list — with each one's type, UCUM unit, attribute keys and stability — can be
read without running a query. The schema comes from one of two places:

  forge obs metrics declared                          # the project's built binary (bin/`<name>`)
  forge obs metrics declared --binary ./bin/api       # a specific binary: runs 'metrics schema --json'
  forge obs metrics declared --url http://localhost:6060   # a running service's pprof listener

The same document is served by 'GET /debug/metrics/schema', printed by
'`<bin>` metrics schema --json', and written by 'forge build' to
bin/observability/metrics-schema.json, which a release carries as
observability/metrics-schema.json.

  forge obs metrics declared --dashboard > deploy/observability/dashboards/`<name>`-custom-metrics.json

prints the HyperDX "Custom metrics" dashboard drawn from the schema (counters as
a rate, histograms as p50/p95/p99, gauges as the last value, grouped by their
enumerated attributes). It writes nothing itself: commit the file to own it.

```
reliant forge obs metrics declared [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--binary` | `string` | - | a built service binary to run (default: the project's bin/`<name>`) |
| `--dashboard` | `bool` | - | print the HyperDX Custom metrics dashboard drawn from the schema instead |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print the forge.metrics/v1 JSON document |
| `--timeout` | `duration` | `30s` | how long to wait for the binary or the service |
| `--url` | `string` | - | a running service's debug listener, e.g. http://localhost:6060 |

#### reliant forge obs metrics describe

One metric: its kind, export interval, attributes and the breakdowns worth trying

What the explorer's side pane shows for one metric: its type, unit, monotonicity
and temporality; the export interval (the floor of a useful --step); every
attribute key with an estimate of how many values it takes, and the declared
closed value set where the schema lists one; the keys worth grouping by; and
what the project's declared schema says about it. The attribute scan reads at
most the last 24 hours of the range. [env] defaults to dev.

  cli forge obs metrics describe dev rpc.server.duration
  cli forge obs metrics describe prod rpc.server.duration --json --fields attributes

#### reliant forge obs metrics exemplars

Sampled observations of a metric, each with the trace it came from

The exemplars of one metric: a sampled observation with the trace that produced
it, newest first, the ones whose trace is still stored before the ones sampled
out. Up to 20, over at most 7 days. [env] defaults to dev.

The exemplars' own timestamps must fall in a window: --at T (with --around, 2m by
default, either side) or --from and --to. Without them it is the whole range.
--min-value keeps the tail of a latency histogram.

  cli forge obs metrics exemplars prod rpc.server.duration --at 2026-10-22T11:40:00Z --min-value 500
  cli forge obs metrics exemplars dev rpc.server.duration --since 1h --where metric.attr.status=error

--where is key=value or key!=value over the metric's attributes (repeatable).
--group KEY=VALUE narrows to one series of a grouped panel: KEY is a --group-by
key of `cli forge obs metrics query` and VALUE that series' label (repeatable, one per key).

  cli forge obs metrics exemplars dev rpc.server.duration --group rpc.method=List --group service.name=api

A stored trace's next command is in "suggested":
  cli forge obs trace `<trace-id>` `<env>`

JSON schema forge.obs.metric_exemplars/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  exemplars
  exemplars.attributes.*
  exemplars.span_id
  exemplars.time
  exemplars.trace_id
  exemplars.trace_stored
  exemplars.value
  metric
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
  window
  window.from
  window.to
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

#### reliant forge obs metrics list

The metrics collected in a window

List the metrics the env's telemetry carries in the window: type (and, for a
counter, whether it is monotonic; for a sum or histogram, its temporality), unit,

written by forge build) are merged in: "declared" says the schema lists it, and
"seen": false is a declared metric with no data in the window.
