#### reliant forge obs metrics query

A metrics panel: one or more metrics over time, as the console's explorer runs it

Run the query the metrics explorer builds: a dashboard panel over metrics. The
positional metric is series A; --query adds more. Locally and on a hosted env the
same evaluator runs it, so the same panel over the same data returns the same
numbers.

  cli forge obs metrics query dev rpc.server.duration --agg p95 --group-by rpc.method
  cli forge obs metrics query dev rpc.server.calls --since 6h --group-by service.name
  cli forge obs metrics query prod rpc.server.calls --agg rate --where A:status=error \
      --query B=rpc.server.calls:rate --formula 'A / B * 100' --kind number --compare 24h
  cli forge obs metrics query dev process.runtime.go.mem.heap_alloc --explain

The aggregation of a series defaults to its kind's: rate for a counter, sum for an
up-down counter, avg for a gauge, p95 for a histogram. --agg is one of
rate, increase, sum, avg, min, max, last, count, quantile (p50, p95 and p99 are the quantiles 0.5, 0.95 and 0.99).

  --query NAME=metric[:agg[:q]]   one more series (repeatable); NAME is its label (B, C, ...)
  --where NAME:key=value          narrow series NAME alone; key!=value excludes (repeatable)
  --filter key=value              narrow every series (repeatable); key!=value excludes
  --group-by KEY                  split by an attribute (repeatable, at most 3)
  --formula EXPR                  plot an expression of the series: 'B / A * 100'
  --kind line|number|table|bar    the shape: a line is a time series; number, table and
                                  bar reduce each series over the whole range
  --top N                         a table or bar keeps the top N groups
  --compare DURATION|prev-release also read the same panel over the range shifted back by
                                  DURATION, or ending when the env's live release was promoted
  --panel-out FILE|-              write the dashboard panel these flags build and run nothing;
                                  `cli forge obs query --panel FILE` runs it, and a dashboard holds it

A key is an attribute of the metric (rpc.method), or service.name, or a
resource.attr.`<key>` / metric.attr.`<key>` spelling. A formula names series by
position: A is the first, B the second. A missing point counts as 0 in a formula
over counters and is a gap over anything else.

JSON schema forge.obs.metrics_query/v2. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  compare
  compare.against
  compare.from
  compare.offset_seconds
  compare.to
  compare_series
  compare_series.label
  compare_series.labels.*
  compare_series.points
  compare_series.points.null
  compare_series.points.partial
  compare_series.points.time
  compare_series.points.value
  compare_series.reduced
  meta
  meta.agg
  meta.label
  meta.metric
  meta.monotonic
  meta.quantile
  meta.temporality
  meta.type
  meta.value_unit
  panel
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  series
  series.label
  series.labels.*
  series.points
  series.points.null
  series.points.partial
  series.points.time
  series.points.value
  series.reduced
  series_total
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  step_seconds
  suggested
  warnings
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs metrics query [env] <metric> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--agg` | `string` | - | aggregation of series A: rate, increase, sum, avg, min, max, last, count, quantile (p50, p95 and p99 are the quantiles 0.5, 0.95 and 0.99) (default: by the metric's kind) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--compare` | `string` | - | also read the range shifted back by this duration, or prev-release |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | narrow every series: key=value or key!=value (repeatable) |
| `--formula` | `string` | - | plot an expression of the series, e.g. 'B / A * 100' |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--group-by` | `stringArray` | `[]` | split by this attribute key (repeatable, at most 3) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--kind` | `string` | - | line (default), number, table or bar |
| `--limit` | `int` | `50` | most series to return per panel series |
| `--panel-out` | `string` | - | write the dashboard panel built from the flags to FILE (- is stdout) and run nothing |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--quantile` | `float64` | `0` | the level of --agg quantile, in (0, 1] |
| `--query` | `stringArray` | `[]` | one more series NAME=metric[:agg[:q]] (repeatable) |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `1h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--step` | `duration` | `0s` | bucket width (default: about 120 points per series, never below the export interval) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |
| `--top` | `int` | `0` | a table or bar keeps the top N groups |
| `--where` | `stringArray` | `[]` | narrow one series NAME:key=value or NAME:key!=value (repeatable) |

#### reliant forge obs profile

Export an aggregated continuous profile as pprof, speedscope or collapsed stacks

Export the continuous profile the local ClickStack collected, for a service
over a time range or for one trace.

Apps push a 10s CPU profile plus heap and goroutine snapshots about once a
minute (pkg/observe, on by default whenever an OTLP endpoint is set). They land
in ClickHouse; HyperDX cannot draw flamegraphs, so this exports them:

  forge obs profile --service api --since 30m -o api.pb.gz
  go tool pprof -http=: api.pb.gz                  # flamegraph, top, source

  forge obs profile --service api --format speedscope -o api.speedscope.json
  #   open api.speedscope.json at https://www.speedscope.app

  forge obs profile --trace 0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c   # CPU burned inside one trace
  forge obs profile --service api --format folded | flamegraph.pl > api.svg

Sample types: cpu (nanoseconds) and inuse_space / inuse_objects (live heap) by
default; alloc_space / alloc_objects and goroutine only from a workload whose
profiling block turns them on (see forge skill load observability/profiles). Raw samples (and so --trace) are kept 24h; the
per-minute aggregate behind --since is kept 30 days. --sql prints the query so
you can run it yourself against the ClickStack ClickHouse.

Needs the local ClickStack running (`forge env up`).

```
reliant forge obs profile [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | - | only samples from this deployment.environment.name |
| `--format` | `string` | `pprof` | pprof | speedscope | folded |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--list` | `bool` | - | list the services that sent profiles in the range |
| `--output`, `-o` | `string` | - | write to this file instead of stdout |
| `--service` | `string` | - | service.name to export (default: the only service that sent profiles) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `1h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--span` | `string` | - | narrow --trace to one span id |
| `--sql` | `bool` | - | print the query and its parameters instead of running it |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |
| `--trace` | `string` | - | export only samples linked to this trace id (raw tier, last 24h) |
| `--type` | `string` | `cpu` | sample type: cpu, alloc_space, inuse_space, alloc_objects, inuse_objects, goroutine |

#### reliant forge obs query

Anything else: raw SQL or a dashboard panel, with the scope bound

Run one read-only SELECT (--sql) or one dashboard panel (--panel FILE) against the
env's telemetry. [env] defaults to dev. This is the escape hatch beside the
structured verbs: when logs / traces / endpoints / metrics cannot say it, say it
in SQL, with the scope already bound.

The statement may use these parameters; forge binds them from the flags, and no
value is ever spliced into the SQL:

  `{from:DateTime64(9)}` `{to:DateTime64(9)}`   the range (--since / --from / --to)
  `{step:UInt32}`                              a bucket width in seconds (--step)
  `{services:Array(String)}`                   the --service values; [] = all
  `{envs:Array(String)}`  `{env_ids:Array(String)}`   the env being read

  cli forge obs query dev --sql "SELECT toStartOfMinute(Timestamp) AS t, count() AS n
      FROM otel_logs WHERE Timestamp >= `{from:DateTime64(9)}` AND Timestamp \< `{to:DateTime64(9)}`
      AND (empty(`{services:Array(String)}`) OR has(`{services:Array(String)}`, ServiceName))
      GROUP BY t ORDER BY t"
  cli forge obs query dev --sql-file slow.sql --param threshold=500
  cli forge obs query dev --panel p95-by-route.json

Start from a structured verb's SQL instead of a blank page:
  cli forge obs endpoints dev --explain          # prints the compiled statement and its parameters

The statement runs through the same guard as an alert rule (one SELECT; no
writes, DDL, SETTINGS, FORMAT, system tables or table functions), read-only,
with a 30s timeout and a row cap (--limit). A refused statement exits 1 with
the reason.

--panel FILE is one dashboard panel as JSON (the panels[] element of a
dashboards/*.json; kinds line, number, table, bar, heatmap, sql) flattened to a
table. Panels over the hosted platform's own tables (issues, sessions, funnel)
need the control plane.

Raw SQL is counted: the JSON document says "query_mode": "raw", and with
FORGE_OBS_DEBUG=1 the statement's shape (literals removed) is logged to stderr.

JSON schema forge.obs.query/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  columns
  columns.name
  columns.type
  query_mode
  rows
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs query [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `200` | most items to return |
| `--panel` | `string` | - | a dashboard panel JSON file |
| `--param` | `stringArray` | `[]` | bind your own `{name:Type}` parameter, name=value (repeatable) |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |

#### reliant forge obs session

What one user did: a text timeline of navigations, clicks, failed requests and errors

Show one browser session as a text timeline, oldest first, with the time since
the session started: the pages the user opened, the elements they clicked (a
masked selector, never the text), the requests that failed (method, path,
status, duration), the errors raised (with the issue each belongs to) and the
custom events the app recorded. Failed requests and errors carry their trace
id, so the next step is `cli forge obs trace <id>`. [env] defaults to dev.

Paths have their query and fragment cut. No recording bytes are printed: the
replay itself is for the console's player.

  cli forge obs session 91b955b313a1a86644ea53730a92deab prod
  cli forge obs session 91b955b313a1a86644ea53730a92deab prod --json

Exit 0 when the session is found, 1 for a bad id or a session the env does not
have (or one older than the 30 days sessions are kept), 2 when it could not be
looked up. A session over --limit entries continues with --page-token. The session is
looked for in the last 30 days; --since, --from and --to narrow that (a
hosted timeline reads at most 7 days).

JSON schema forge.obs.session/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  query_mode
  scope

  session.has_replay
  session.id
  session.interactions
  session.page_views
  session.service
  session.started
  session.user_id
  session.version
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
  timeline
  timeline.duration_ms
  timeline.element

  timeline.fingerprint
  timeline.issue_id

  timeline.message
  timeline.method

  timeline.status
  timeline.time
  timeline.trace_id

#### reliant forge obs sessions

Real-user browser sessions: who hit errors, and which have a replay

List the env's browser sessions that started in the range, newest first: the
frontend (APP) and start time, user, entry path, length, page views,
interactions, browser errors, whether a replay was recorded, and the session id
(last column). [env] defaults to dev; --since defaults to 24h (at most 30d).

Read one with `cli forge obs session <id>`: a text timeline of what the user did.

  cli forge obs sessions prod --has-error
  cli forge obs sessions prod --user user-42 --since 7d
  cli forge obs sessions prod --path /checkout --has-replay --json

Sessions are derived by the control plane from the browser's telemetry: a local
ClickStack has no session tables, so a local env answers "could not look".

#### reliant forge obs status

What is wrong right now? Services, open issues, alerts, telemetry and the release in force

One look at [env] (default dev) over --since (default 1h):

  services         requests, error rate and latency per service, busiest first
  issues           the new and regressed issues (locally: every error group)
  alerts           the alerts firing or pending (hosted envs)
  telemetry        when the newest log and span arrived; hosted, whether the
                   control plane's telemetry token reached the cluster
  current_release  the promotion in force, and the last few changes

Each section is read on its own: one the backend cannot answer is listed in
`unread` with the reason in `warnings`, and the rest are still shown. The exit
status is 2 when any section is unread, with the readable evidence preserved.
A complete read exits 0; this is not a health verdict (use
`cli forge obs check` to gate a release).

  cli forge obs status prod
  cli forge obs status dev --since 15m --service api
  cli forge obs status prod --json --fields services,issues.id,issues.title

#### reliant forge obs trace

One trace as a tree, with its errors and logs

Show one trace: its span tree, the failing spans with their exception, and the
logs that carry its id. [env] defaults to dev.

Each span line carries what says how it ended: the rpc / http outcome,
error.type, a database statement's summary, the rows a ClickHouse statement
read and its query_id (join system.query_log on it). A log that failed or
warned is shown under its span with its error and cause. A span whose children
cover little of its time gets an "uninstrumented" line: the time went to code
that emitted no span, which is where to look next.

The tree is capped for an agent's context: depth 8, 100 rendered spans, runs of
identical siblings collapsed ("×37 SELECT jobs … p50 2ms p95 9ms"); failing
spans, their ancestors and the critical path (the child that ends last at each
level, marked *) are always expanded. --full lifts the caps.

  cli forge obs trace 0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c dev
  cli forge obs trace 0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c dev --full --json

JSON schema forge.obs.trace/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  capped
  duration_ms
  errors
  errors.exception
  errors.message
  errors.name
  errors.service
  errors.span_id
  logs
  logs.attributes.*
  logs.body
  logs.service
  logs.severity
  logs.span_id
  logs.time
  logs_truncated
  orphans
  query_mode
  release
  release.promoted_at
  release.promotion_id
  release.unsettled
  release.version
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.to
  spans_shown
  spans_total
  start
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
  trace_id
  tree
  tree.attributes.*
  tree.children
  tree.count
  tree.critical
  tree.duration_ms
  tree.error
  tree.gap_from_ms
  tree.gap_to_ms
  tree.hidden

  tree.kind

| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--full` | `bool` | - | lift the depth, span and collapse caps |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--logs` | `int` | `20` | most correlated log lines to include (0 = none) |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `72h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

---

#### reliant forge obs traces

Find traces: the slowest, the failing, by route

List the env's traces, newest first, with a rate / errors / duration summary over
every trace the filters match. [env] defaults to dev.

  cli forge obs traces dev --errors                      # traces with an error span
  cli forge obs traces dev --slow --min-duration 500ms   # slowest first
  cli forge obs traces dev --route /v1/jobs --service api
  cli forge obs traces dev --errors --explain            # the compiled SQL

--slow orders by duration within the newest 500 matching traces (the compiler
pages newest-first only); sorted_from says how many were sorted.

JSON schema forge.obs.traces/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  sorted_from
  statements
  statements.params.*
  statements.purpose
  statements.sql
  suggested
  summary
  summary.error_traces
  summary.p50_ms
  summary.p95_ms
  summary.p99_ms
  summary.traces
  traces
  traces.duration_ms
  traces.errors
  traces.root_duration_ms
  traces.root_name
  traces.root_service
  traces.services
  traces.spans
  traces.start
  traces.trace_id
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs traces [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--errors` | `bool` | - | only traces with an error span |
| `--explain` | `bool` | - | print the compiled SQL and its parameters instead of running it (a starting point for obs query --sql) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--filter` | `stringArray` | `[]` | attribute predicate key=value or key!=value over the allowlisted keys (repeatable) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `50` | most items to return |
| `--min-duration` | `duration` | `0s` | only traces at least this long, e.g. 500ms |
| `--page-token` | `string` | - | resume from a next_page_token |
| `--print-sql` | `bool` | - | alias of --explain |
| `--route` | `string` | - | only traces whose root span name contains this |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `1h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--slow` | `bool` | - | slowest first |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

#### reliant forge obs workloads

Restarts, crash loops, last termination reasons and failing jobs, per workload

Show each workload of [env] (default dev) over the range: its health verdict
(crash_looping, degraded, pending, unknown, healthy, suspended) and why, ready
and desired replicas, restarts, the last termination reason (OOMKilled,
Error, ...) and the latest Kubernetes warning; then the env's Job runs
(migrations, provisioning, backups), failed ones first. Read through the
control plane from the cluster's own state, so it needs no kubeconfig.

By default only workloads that need a look are listed (not healthy, or
restarted in the range); --all lists every one.

  cli forge obs workloads prod
  cli forge obs workloads prod --since 72h --all
  cli forge obs workloads prod --service api --json
  cli forge obs workloads prod --jobs all

The range is at most 7 days. Exit 0 when the read succeeded, whatever it
found; 2 when it could not look.

JSON schema forge.obs.workloads/v1. --fields takes dotted paths of:
  jobs
  jobs.duration_seconds
  jobs.finished_at
  jobs.name
  jobs.reason
  jobs.release
  jobs.result
  jobs.started_at
  jobs.workload
  jobs_truncated
  query_mode
  scope
  scope.backend
  scope.env
  scope.env_id
  scope.from
  scope.services
  scope.to
  suggested
  summary
  summary.crash_looping
  summary.failed_jobs
  summary.restarts
  summary.unhealthy
  summary.workloads
  truncated
  workloads
  workloads.cause
  workloads.containers
  workloads.desired
  workloads.detail
  workloads.health
  workloads.kind
  workloads.last_exit_at
  workloads.last_exit_reason
  workloads.latest_warning
  workloads.namespace
  workloads.ready
  workloads.reason
  workloads.release
  workloads.restarts
  workloads.since
  workloads.workload
Always kept: schema, ok, exit_code, error, truncated, omitted, next_page_token.

```
reliant forge obs workloads [env] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--all` | `bool` | - | list every workload, healthy ones too |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--backend` | `string` | - | local | cloud (default: from the env's declaration) |
| `--fields` | `string` | - | with --json: keep only these comma-separated dotted fields (the head is always kept) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | range start, RFC 3339 |
| `--jobs` | `string` | `failed` | which Job runs to list: failed | all | none |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | print one JSON document on stdout (see the schema below; progress goes to stderr) |
| `--service` | `stringArray` | `[]` | only this service.name (repeatable) |
| `--since` | `duration` | `24h0m0s` | how far back to read (ignored with --from) |
| `--to` | `string` | - | range end, RFC 3339 (default now) |

### reliant forge package

Manage internal packages

Manage internal packages with Go interface contracts.

Internal packages live under internal/`<name>`/ and define their boundary
through a Go interface in contract.go. Unlike proto API services, internal
package contracts use native Go interfaces — supporting channels, complex
types, factories, and other constructs that proto cannot express.

Subcommands:
  forge package new `<name>`   Create a new internal package

```
reliant forge package [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`new`](#reliant-forge-package-new) | Create a new internal package with contract interface |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge package new

Create a new internal package with contract interface

Create a new internal package under internal/`<name>`/ with:
  - contract.go  — Go interface that IS the package contract
  - service.go   — Implementation with unexported concrete type

It ends with a 'forge generate' run, which writes mock_gen.go and
middleware_gen.go and wires the package into internal/app/compose.go, so
the project lints clean straight away (--no-generate skips it). After you
add methods to contract.go, run 'forge generate' again.

package shape: service|adapter (default service)

  service     Standard internal/`<name>`/ with Service/Deps/New — wired into
              the composition, callable by handlers. The default, and the
              right answer for a use-case orchestrator too: an orchestrator
              is a service whose Deps are other services' interfaces.
  adapter     The same shape plus '// forge:outbound-io', which asserts the
              package calls OUT to a third-party system and serves nothing
              inbound. Lint keeps RPC handlers out of it and the observe
              heuristic treats it as doing I/O.
              See: forge skill load adapter

Example:
  forge package new cache
  forge package new notifications
  forge package new events --kind eventbus
  forge package new stripe-adapter --type adapter --base-url
  forge package new pubsub --type adapter --dry-run

```
reliant forge package new <name> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--base-url` | `bool` | - | adapter only: compose a config block with a base_url leaf, bound to `<NAME>`_BASE_URL; omit for an adapter with no single base URL |
| `--base-url-env` | `string` | - | adapter only: the `ENV_VAR` the base_url leaf binds instead of `<NAME>`_BASE_URL (implies --base-url) |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | print the files and config edits the scaffold would make, and write nothing |
| `--kind` | `string` | - | package kind template (e.g. eventbus, client) |
| `--no-generate` | `bool` | - | skip the post-scaffold 'forge generate' run (scaffold-only mode for parallel-agent rounds); run 'forge generate' before 'forge lint' |
| `--type` | `string` | `service` | package shape: service|adapter (see --help for details) |

Create, evolve, and inspect the project as a whole

| [`disown`](#reliant-forge-project-disown) | Permanently transfer a forge-generated file to user ownership (one-way), or decline a scaffold (--absent) |

| [`introspect`](#reliant-forge-project-introspect) | Inspect what the assembled binary will expose at runtime |

| [`map`](#reliant-forge-project-map) | Print project tree with ownership annotations |

| [`new`](#reliant-forge-project-new) | Create a new Forge project (service / CLI / library / static site) |
| [`rescaffold`](#reliant-forge-project-rescaffold) | Re-create scaffold-once files you deleted, as forge would scaffold them today |

#### reliant forge project annotations

Dump forge's authoritative entity-authoring annotation spec

Dump forge's authoritative annotation spec.

Emits the vocabulary forge itself understands, sourced from forge's own
definitions so it cannot drift from behavior:

  markers          the // forge:* comment markers, in proto AND Go source,
                   plus the forge:* COMMENT ON COLUMN/CONSTRAINT markers
  field_types      the proto->column mapping an entity birth applies
  validate_rules   the projected protovalidate subset and its db/zod/wire effects
  service_options  the (forge.v1.service) options
  method_options   the (forge.v1.method) options

Use --kind to limit the dump to one annotation level, and --json for a
machine-readable dump that tools can query instead of re-deriving the spec.
--kind column is the forge:* markers declared as a postgres catalog COMMENT
in a migration rather than a proto/Go comment (forge:immutable on a column,
forge:ref on a foreign-key constraint); --kind table is the same mechanism one
level up, on the table itself (forge:append-only). --kind go is the wiring/observability
/contract vocabulary forge reads out of .go files (forge:optional-dep,
forge:constructor, forge:no-observe, forge:exclude-contract, ...) — the
markers you meet in scaffolded code.

Examples:
  forge project annotations --json
  forge project annotations --kind entity --json
  forge project annotations --kind column
  forge project annotations --kind go

```
reliant forge project annotations [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | emit the spec as JSON |
| `--kind` | `string` | - | limit to one annotation kind: entity, field, column, table, service, method, or go (default: all) |

---

#### reliant forge project audit

Print a comprehensive project state snapshot

Print a comprehensive snapshot of forge project state.

Audit reports forge version pin, project shape, lint roll-ups, codegen
state, proto vs migration alignment, scaffold markers, and dep health.
Use --json for machine-readable output (sub-agents).

EXIT CODE. Audit exits non-zero when a category reports an ERROR (✗), and
zero when the worst finding is a warning (⚠). Warnings are reported and
never gate: a freshly-scaffolded project legitimately carries several, so
failing on them would make forge's own output fail forge's own gate.

Categories that can error are ones you armed. unscoped_auth is the
clearest case: it warns about authenticated RPCs that never resolve the
caller, and becomes an error only for RPCs over a table whose migration
declares a forge:owner column — your sentence, in your schema, is what
turns the advice into a gate.

Examples:
  forge project audit            # human-readable; exits 1 on any ✗
  forge project audit --json     # machine-readable (same exit code)

```
reliant forge project audit [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Output as JSON |

---

#### reliant forge project capabilities

List everything forge can do — every command, analyzer, and marker, in one call

List everything forge can do, in one call.

Emits forge's whole surface, enumerated from forge itself:

  commands    every verb in the command tree, with its one-line summary
  analyzers   every 'forge lint' analyzer, including the maintainer flags
              hidden from --help
  markers     every // forge:* comment marker the proto scanner reads

Read this BEFORE hand-writing something forge already scaffolds. Two
sibling dumps cover the rest of the vocabulary:

  forge project annotations   the entity-authoring spec (the proto->column
                              mapping, projected buf.validate rules,
                              proto options)
  forge skill list            the skill catalog; 'forge skill load `<name>`'
                              prints the copy THIS binary ships

Examples:
  forge project capabilities
  forge project capabilities --json

```
reliant forge project capabilities [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | emit the inventory as JSON |

#### reliant forge project checkouts

List origin/main plus every git worktree — the Preview picker's source

List the checkouts of this project: `origin/<main>` plus every git worktree,
each with its branch, HEAD, dirty flag, dev-stack key, and how far it is from
main.

WHAT IT IS FOR. Preview renders an arbitrary checkout and diffs it against
Live, so something has to offer the choice. The reliant daemon runs this and
the picker shows the result — and because the daemon accepts only a path this
command returned, the list is also the allowlist that keeps the UI from
pointing forge at an arbitrary directory.

"main" MEANS `origin/<main>`, NOT your local main branch. That is what a
protected environment is judged against, and a local main that has not been
fetched is a different tree. The remote entry carries no path, because there
is nothing on disk to render until someone checks it out.

GIT ONLY, AND FAST. No tree hash by default: hashing one checkout costs about
half a second, so hashing twenty would make the picker unusable to answer a
question nobody has asked yet. `--tree` hashes exactly one — the checkout you
are in — which is the only one whose cache key is about to be needed.

ahead/behind are OMITTED rather than zero when the comparison cannot be made
(no origin, or main never fetched). "In step with main" and "I could not tell"
are different answers.

Examples:
  cli forge project checkouts
  cli forge project checkouts --json          # the form the daemon's hook returns
  cli forge project checkouts --json --tree   # plus the tree hash of this checkout

```
reliant forge project checkouts [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print the checkouts as JSON |
| `--tree` | `bool` | - | Also compute the tree hash of the checkout you are in (adds ~0.5s) |

#### reliant forge project delete

Delete (retire) a service from the project — the inverse of `forge scaffold service`

Delete a component from an existing Forge project.

Subcommands:
  forge project delete service `<name>`   Retire a service: remove the handlers/`<name>`/
                                scaffold and leave a types-only tombstone in
                                pkg/app/services.go so the proto types / Connect
                                client keep generating for callers while the
                                handler is no longer served.

```
reliant forge project delete [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`service`](#reliant-forge-project-delete-service) | Retire a service (inverse of `forge scaffold service`) |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge project delete service

Retire a service (inverse of `forge scaffold service`)

Retire a service from this project.

What it does:
  - deletes the handlers/`<name>`/ scaffold directory
  - leaves a types-only tombstone comment in pkg/app/services.go (so the
    proto types, Connect client, and frontend hooks keep generating for
    callers — the service is no longer SERVED, but its contract survives)

Pass --no-keep-types to omit the tombstone comment entirely; the service
then reverts to "unlisted" and forge will re-scaffold its handler on the
next generate if it's still declared in proto.

This is destructive (it removes a directory). It prompts for confirmation
unless --yes; --dry-run prints the plan and changes nothing.

Example:
  forge project delete service reporting

```
reliant forge project delete service <name> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print what would change without touching any files |
| `--keep-types` | `bool` | `true` | Leave a types-only tombstone in pkg/app/services.go so proto types + Connect client keep generating (default; --keep-types=false to fully unlist) |
| `--yes` | `bool` | - | Skip the confirmation prompt |

---

#### reliant forge project disown

Permanently transfer a forge-generated file to user ownership (one-way), or decline a scaffold (--absent)

Transfer one or more forge-owned (Tier-1) generated files to user ownership.

After disowning, forge NEVER touches the file again: no regeneration, no
overwrite (not even with --force), no drift errors. The file is yours, like any
starter scaffold. This is a ONE-WAY door — there is no "un-disown" that keeps
your edits.

--reason is required. Disowning means the generated code couldn't express what
you needed; the reason is design feedback, recorded per path in
.forge/disowned.json (surfaced by `forge project audit --json`).

Re-adoption (returning the file to forge ownership) is by deletion:

  rm `<path>` && forge generate

The emitter re-emits the pristine render and the entry returns to Tier-1. Your
disowned content is discarded — copy anything you want to keep into a
user-owned extension point first.

Prefer NOT disowning when you can: most customizations have a designated
user-owned home (pkg/app/setup.go / app_extras.go,
…) that survives every regenerate.

--absent declines a SCAFFOLD-ONCE file instead: forge never writes it in this
project. Use it for a scaffold the project does not want at all — one whose
very presence would change the build (a cmd tree's serve.go switches internal/app
composition on) — including one forge has not written yet, or one a failed
generate wrote and then rolled back. The decline is recorded with its reason in
.forge/scaffolded.json, which a failed `forge generate` restores rather than
loses. Re-adopt it with `forge project rescaffold <path>`.

Examples:
  forge project disown pkg/app/wire_gen.go --reason "custom connection-pool wiring forge can't express"
  forge project disown cmd/app/cmd/serve.go cmd/app/cmd/metrics.go --absent --reason "serve.go turns composition on; our roots land later"

```
reliant forge project disown <path>... --reason <text> [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--absent` | `bool` | - | Decline scaffold-once files: forge never writes them in this project (recorded in .forge/scaffolded.json) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print what would change without writing .forge/disowned.json |
| `--reason` | `string` | - | WHY forge's generated code couldn't express what you needed (required; recorded in .forge/disowned.json, or .forge/scaffolded.json with --absent) |

---

#### reliant forge project features

Print the resolved feature graph (on/off, why, dependencies)

Print every forge feature for this project: whether it is enabled,
WHY (what in the repo made it on or off), and the features / shape
preconditions it depends on. Every feature is derived from what exists in
the repository, unless forge.yaml's features: block pins it on or off
(features: `{observability: false}`). The ORM has no key: //forge:no-orm in
internal/db switches it off.

The dependency graph is validated at config-load time — a feature
enabled with a dependency off is a load error. This command shows the
resolved, coherent set: codegen, orm, migrations, frontend, deploy,
ingress, and the rest, with their edges.

```
reliant forge project features [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

#### reliant forge project frontends

Print the directories of this project's in-repo frontends, one per line (what task and the CI gate loop over)

Print the project-relative directory of every frontend in this repository,
one per line, sorted by name.

The list is the one `forge ci gate` and the scaffolded workflows use: each
frontend a forge.Frontend declaration in deploy/kcl/`<env>`/main.k names (any
env, at any in-repo path), plus the frontends/`<name>` directories on disk. A
frontend outside the repository (a sibling checkout, a cross-repo source pin)
is not listed: nothing here can install or build it.

Scripts loop over it rather than globbing frontends/*:

  for dir in $(forge project frontends); do (cd "$dir" && npm test); done

--json prints `[{"name","path"}]` instead.
--with-package-manager prints manager:path rows using forge's install/CI
resolver, including the declared manager's own lockfile when several exist.

```
reliant forge project frontends [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print `[{name, path}]` as JSON |
| `--with-package-manager` | `bool` | - | Print manager:path rows using the same resolver as installs and CI |

#### reliant forge project graph

Emit a JSON dependency graph of the project's declared resources

Emit a single JSON document describing every resource the
project declares (services, packages, frontends, binaries, gateways,
routes) and the explicit dependency edges between them.

Sources are existing forge parsers: forge.yaml, the KCL render for
the selected env, contract.go's Deps struct, and gen/forge_descriptor.json.
Partial-data conditions (missing forge.yaml, KCL fails to render) are
reported via the top-level "warnings" array rather than aborting the
command — consumers should always read warnings before trusting the
graph as complete.

Output goes to stdout; warnings and errors to stderr.

Examples:
  forge project graph                 # dev env
  forge project graph --env=staging   # staging KCL render

```
reliant forge project graph [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--env` | `string` | `dev` | KCL environment to render |

#### reliant forge project libraries

Print forge/pkg's API — name a package to get its full signatures

Print forge's public runtime libraries, and the full API of any you name.

With NO arguments this is an index: forge ships TWO runtime libraries and it
prints both — forge/pkg for Go and @reliantlabs/forge-web-runtime for the frontend
— one line per package, from each package's own doc comment.

NAME A PACKAGE and it stops being an index and answers the question:

  forge project libraries crud             every signature crud exports
  forge project libraries crud orm svcerr  three packages, one call
  forge project libraries orm.Context      one type, with its methods
  forge project libraries all              everything (large — prefer a list)

That prints every func with its parameters, every struct with its fields,
every interface and type with its methods, parsed out of the source this
project actually resolves. Doc prose is omitted, so the block is API and
nothing else.

Use this INSTEAD of 'go doc `<pkg>`', which cannot answer the same question:
'go doc' renders a struct or interface as '`struct{ ... }`' and lists no
methods at all, so 'go doc .../crud' never mentions crud.Repo.UpdateMasked.
'go doc -all `<pkg>`' is complete but roughly ten times larger, most of it
prose. Neither needs the source directory, and neither does reading files.

A selector naming a package or symbol that does not exist is an error listing
what does, so a briefing script whose list has gone stale fails loudly rather
than quietly shipping an incomplete API.

Examples:
  forge project libraries
  forge project libraries --json
  forge project libraries svcerr crud tdd testkit orm.Context

```
reliant forge project libraries [package...] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | emit the inventory as JSON |
| `--signatures` | `string` | - | same selectors as the positional form, comma-separated: `pkg`, `pkg.Symbol`, or `all` |

#### reliant forge project migrate import

Import migrations from another format (e.g. goose) into golang-migrate shape

Import SQL migrations from another tool's format into forge's
forward-only golang-migrate shape (one .up.sql per migration).

Currently supports:
  --from goose    One-file goose migrations with -- +goose Up / -- +goose Down

For each *.sql file in --src-dir, the importer:
  1. Keeps the -- +goose Up section and DROPS the -- +goose Down section.
     Forge rolls forward only and never runs down SQL, so the importer
     writes no .down.sql; each dropped Down section is listed so you can
     see what was discarded.
  2. Drops -- +goose StatementBegin / -- +goose StatementEnd markers.
  3. Splits a -- +goose NO TRANSACTION file into one migration per goose
     statement. goose ran each of those statements on its own, outside any
     transaction. golang-migrate runs a file as ONE multi-statement query,
     which postgres runs as one implicit transaction, so two
     CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY statements in one file fail with SQLSTATE
     25001 and leave the schema dirty. One statement per file runs exactly
     as goose ran it.
  4. Renumbers starting from the next-available index in --dest-dir, so
     pack-installed migrations (00001-0000N) keep their slots.

Files with no goose markers are skipped.

Importing renumbers the history, so it suits a project whose databases can be
rebuilt. A database goose ALREADY migrated would run every imported migration
again. For those, squash goose's history into one baseline whose header
declares it — "-- forge:adopts goose \<every goose version>" — and the migrator
adopts each goose database on its next run without re-running anything. See
the forge skill db/deploy-migrations, "Adopting a database another tool
migrated".

Examples:
  forge project migrate import --from goose --src-dir ../old-project/migrations
  forge project migrate import --from goose --src-dir ./legacy --dry-run
  forge project migrate import --from goose --src-dir ./legacy --force

```
reliant forge project migrate import [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--dest-dir` | `string` | - | Destination migrations directory (default: project's migrations dir) |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print planned writes without touching disk |
| `--force` | `bool` | - | Overwrite existing target files (default refuses) |
| `--from` | `string` | - | Source format (currently only 'goose' supported) |
| `--src-dir` | `string` | - | Directory containing source migration files |

#### reliant forge project migrate tdd

Rewrite hand-rolled handler tests to use tdd.RunRPCCases

Codemod hand-rolled tests := []`struct{name, call}``{...}` Connect-RPC
test scaffolds into per-RPC TestXxx_Generated functions that delegate to
forge/pkg/tdd.RunRPCCases.

Walks every *_test.go file under handlers/`<svc>`/ in the project root (or

Files that don't match are skipped with a clear reason and never partially

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|

| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Print actions without writing files |
| `--path` | `string` | - | Project root or subtree to walk (default: cwd) |

#### reliant forge project new

Create a new Forge project (service / CLI / library / static site)

Create a new project with the Forge framework structure.

By default no service is scaffolded: the binary is a deployment unit
that mounts services — it is not a domain entity. Add your first
service after scaffolding with 'forge scaffold service `<entity>`' (name it
after a domain entity like item/order/user, not the binary), or opt
into an initial service at creation time with --service `<entity>`.

Pick a project kind with --kind:

  --kind service  (default) Connect-RPC service: handlers, middleware, deploy
                            manifests, observability wiring, frontend support.
  --kind cli                Cobra-based CLI binary: cmd/`<name>`/main.go +
                            cmd/`<name>`/version.go, no server scaffolding,
                            no proto/services, no deploy/.
  --kind library            Pure Go module: pkg/`<name>`/ skeleton, no cmd/,
                            no CI workflows by default.
  --kind site               A static site — a landing page, marketing or docs
                            site, SPA — with no backend: the frontend at the
                            project root plus forge.yaml and deploy/kcl
                            dev/staging/prod envs, bound to hosted static
                            hosting. No Go module, no server. A new site gets
                            a starter app (--frontend-kind nextjs, the default:
                            a static export; or vite-spa); --in-place inside an
                            existing Vite or Next.js app adopts it untouched.

Use --disable to turn features off at creation time. Each is pinned off in
forge.yaml's features: block, so a later change to the tree does not turn it
back on (orm has no key; --disable orm only leaves db/migrations out):
  forge project new my-project --mod ... --disable ci,deploy
  forge project new my-project --mod ... --disable orm --disable migrations

Valid feature names: orm, codegen, migrations, ci, build, deploy,
contracts, docs, frontend, observability, hot_reload.

Example:
  forge project new my-project --mod github.com/example/my-project
  forge project new my-project --mod github.com/example/my-project --service gateway
  forge project new my-project --mod github.com/example/my-project --frontend web
  forge project new mycli      --mod github.com/example/mycli --kind cli
  forge project new mylib      --mod github.com/example/mylib --kind library
  forge project new landing    --kind site                          # new static site (Next.js export)
  forge project new landing    --kind site --frontend-kind vite-spa # new static site (Vite SPA)
  cd my-vite-app && forge project new --in-place --kind site --name landing   # adopt an existing site
  forge project new --in-place --mod github.com/example/my-project
  forge project new --in-place --name my-project --mod github.com/example/my-project

With --in-place and no name, the project is named after the DIRECTORY. That
name becomes cmd/`<name>`/, the binary, the image and the deploy manifests, so
in a worktree or a branch checkout — where the directory is named after the
branch rather than the product — pass --name.

--in-place never overwrites a file that already exists: forge keeps yours,
skips its own version, and lists what it kept (--force replaces them). An
existing .gitignore is merged — forge appends only the entries it lacks,
in a "# --- forge ---" block. A directory already inside a git repository
(its root or any subdirectory) is left alone: no git init, no commit. A
fresh directory outside any repository gets 'git init' + an initial commit.

--link-forge bridges the new project to the forge checkout THIS binary was
built from: a gitignored, machine-local go.work 'use', plus the npm twin for
@reliantlabs/forge-web-runtime (.forge-link/). The project then compiles the
library from that checkout instead of a published version — what a forge
contributor wants, and something nobody else should get by accident, so it
is written only when asked for or when nothing else works: an unreleased
forge build cannot pin itself in go.mod, so a service scaffold from one is
linked to the checkout that build came from automatically (one line says
so), and refused when that checkout cannot be found or with
--link-forge=false. A linked project's forge_version follows the checkout,
not go.mod's placeholder require. Once
bridged, generate, lint and doctor warn whenever this binary and the
checkout stop being the same source; drop the bridge with
'go work edit -dropuse=`<checkout>`'.

```
reliant forge project new [project-name] --mod [module-path] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--binary` | `string` | `per-service` | Binary packaging: 'per-service' (default — canonical cmd/server.go cobra root, one Application per service) or 'shared' (one Go binary, cobra subcommand per service, KCL MultiServiceApplication for deploy) |
| `--buf-plugins` | `string` | `local` | Default proto plugin source: 'local' (resolved from PATH; no BSR auth needed) or 'remote' (BSR-hosted, requires login under load) |
| `--claimable` | `bool` | - | Scaffold prod for an ANONYMOUS deploy: forge env deploy prod --claimable then publishes with no account and prints a claim link. prod declares control_plane = forge.ControlPlane `{claimable = True}` and sizes its hosted services and jobs to the claimable plan (125m / 128 MiB, request and limit) instead of the billed shape band. A static site needs nothing extra |
| `--disable` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Features to leave out of the scaffold (comma-separated): orm, codegen, migrations, ci, build, deploy, contracts, frontend, observability, hot_reload. Each is pinned off in forge.yaml's features: block (orm, which has no key, only steers this scaffold). |
| `--force` | `bool` | - | With --in-place: scaffold over an existing forge.yaml, and REPLACE every pre-existing file the scaffold writes (README.md, go.mod, Taskfile.yml, …) with forge's version. Without it, existing files are kept and listed. .gitignore is always merged, never replaced |
| `--forge-root` | `string` | - | The forge checkout --link-forge bridges to (also: FORGE_ROOT). Default: the checkout this binary was built from, else the go.mod replace it embeds forge through, else the checkout it sits or was started in |
| `--frontend` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Name(s) of Next.js frontends (can be repeated or comma-separated). With --kind site: the one name the site's frontend is declared under (default web) |
| `--frontend-kind` | `string` | - | With --kind site: the starter a new site gets — nextjs (default; a static export, one HTML file per route) or vite-spa (a single-page app) |
| `--frontend-workspaces` | `bool` | - | Opt into pnpm-workspaces layout: emit packages/api + packages/hooks + packages/ui-web shared across all frontends. Off by default; recommended once you have 2+ frontends (web + mobile). |
| `--go-version` | `string` | - | Go version to use in go.mod (e.g., 1.24); defaults to detected version |
| `--harness` | `string` | - | AI harness to scaffold for: reliant, claude, cursor, copilot or codex. Default: detected from the agent running this command (RELIANT_AGENT_SHELL, CLAUDECODE, CODEX_THREAD_ID, CURSOR_AGENT), else reliant. Every harness gets AGENTS.md, the project guide; claude adds CLAUDE.md (imports AGENTS.md) and a .claude/skills/forge pointer skill, cursor adds .cursorrules and .cursor/rules/forge.mdc, copilot adds .github/copilot-instructions.md, reliant renders the guide in memory from forge.yaml. The pointers hold no skill bodies: skills are read from the forge binary ('forge skill search|load'). Recorded as 'harness:' in forge.yaml and honored by every later generate |
| `--in-place` | `bool` | - | Create project in current directory instead of a new subdirectory |
| `--kind` | `string` | - | Project kind: service (default), cli, library, site (a static site with no backend: a new starter app, or with --in-place the Vite/Next.js app already here) |
| `--link-forge` | `bool` | - | Bridge the project to the forge checkout this binary was built from (gitignored go.work 'use' + .forge-link/ for the web runtime). Off by default, except for a service scaffold from an unreleased forge build, which is linked automatically when its checkout is found (--link-forge=false refuses instead; also: FORGE_LINK_FORGE=1) |
| `--mod` | `string` | - | Go module path (e.g., github.com/example/my-project). Defaults to example.com/`<name>`, so a first project needs no flag; set it to the path you will publish under |
| `--name` | `string` | - | Project name. Same as the positional arg, and the way to name an --in-place project whose directory (a worktree, a branch checkout) is not the product name; defaults to the directory name |
| `--output` | `string` | - | Output for every --frontend: static (default), standalone, or server |
| `--path`, `-p` | `string` | `.` | Path where to create the project |
| `--routes` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | CRUD route allowlist for every --frontend; none generates no CRUD pages |
| `--service` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Name(s) of initial Go services (repeatable or comma-separated). Name services after domain entities (item, order), not the binary. Omit to scaffold zero services and add them later via 'forge scaffold service `<entity>`' |
| `--skip-tools` | `bool` | - | Skip auto-installing protoc-gen-go / protoc-gen-connect-go (run 'forge tools install' later) |
| `--verbose`, `-v` | `bool` | - | Print the raw output behind a step forge summarizes in one line (e.g. git's explanation when the initial commit is skipped for want of a git identity) |
| `--verify` | `bool` | - | Compile the scaffold before returning: the bootstrap generate then ends with its go build check and a render of every deploy env (adds ~1s warm, ~40s on a cold build cache, and saturates the CPU while it runs). Off by default: the scaffold is deterministic and forge's own e2e corpus compiles it, and your first go build or forge generate pays for the same compile |

---

#### reliant forge project rescaffold

Re-create scaffold-once files you deleted, as forge would scaffold them today

Re-create one or more scaffold-once ("yours") files.

forge writes a scaffold-once file exactly once. Deleting it is an act of
ownership that .forge/scaffolded.json records, so `forge generate` leaves it deleted —
in this checkout and every clone. To get forge's version back, name it here:

  forge project rescaffold .github/workflows/ci.yml

For each path, rescaffold drops its birth record, writes the file the way forge
scaffolds it for this project today, and records the birth again: the file is
yours once more, and deleting it again sticks.

Every file forge scaffolds can be re-created this way — CI workflows (through
the same mapper `forge generate` uses, so only workflows this project has),
.pre-commit-config.yaml, the devcontainer, the command tree, internal/app, the
frontend pages, and the rest.

A path that still exists is refused: rescaffold never overwrites your bytes. To
replace a file you have edited with the current template, use
`forge project upgrade --force <path>`, which shows the diff first.

Examples:
  forge project rescaffold .github/workflows/e2e.yml .pre-commit-config.yaml
  forge project rescaffold internal/handlers/item/handlers_crud_test.go

```
reliant forge project rescaffold <path>... [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

---

#### reliant forge project scaffolded

List the scaffold-once files forge has written, and which are absent

List every scaffold-once ("yours") file forge has written in this project,
from the birth ledger at .forge/scaffolded.json.

forge writes a scaffold-once file exactly once and then it is yours. Deleting
one is an act of ownership the ledger records, so `forge generate` leaves it
deleted — here and in every clone. `forge generate` reports that set only when
it CHANGES; this command answers it any time.

  present   the file is on disk — your bytes, forge leaves them alone
  ABSENT    you deleted it, and forge is respecting that

To have forge write an absent one again, fresh against the project as it
stands today:

  cli forge project rescaffold `<path>`

Examples:
  cli forge project scaffolded
  cli forge project scaffolded --absent

```
reliant forge project scaffolded [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--absent` | `bool` | - | List only the files that are absent (deleted on purpose) |
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |

#### reliant forge project shapes

List every API shape — RPCs, messages, enums, tables, handlers, hooks — with file:line

List every API shape in the project with its source location.

Derived LIVE from .proto, db/migrations and source on every call — never from
gen/forge_descriptor.json, which is a cache and can be stale. Answers "where is
X and what shape is it" in ONE call instead of a sequence of greps.

Start recon here. A measured fan-out spent 28 minutes across 123 calls
re-deriving this by hand, mostly paging one large proto by symbol.

--grep is a REGEX, so ask about every entity you own in ONE call. A later run
adopted this command and still invoked it once per entity — 11 turns of
--grep Property then --grep Customer — which is the old grep loop wearing a
new command's name.

Examples:
  forge project shapes                          # everything
  forge project shapes --grep Estimate          # one entity across all layers
  forge project shapes --grep 'Invoice|Payment' # SEVERAL entities in one call
  forge project shapes --kind rpc,handler       # what is declared vs implemented
  forge project shapes --service ChatService    # one service's rpcs and their handlers
  forge project shapes --unimplemented          # declared rpcs with no handler (or a stub)
  forge project shapes --kind deploy-target     # what a workload's deploy= accepts

A handler line is named like the rpc it serves (ChatService.SendMessage), so
rpc and handler lines diff by name. Only methods on a handler package's
*Service that ARE an rpc of its service are handlers: helpers, Name and
Register are not listed.

```
reliant forge project shapes [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--allow-version-skew` | `bool` | - | Run even though this forge is not the forge_version forge.yaml pins (also FORGE_ALLOW_VERSION_SKEW=1) |
| `--grep` | `string` | - | case-insensitive regex filter over name and detail |
| `--kind` | `string` | - | comma-separated kinds: rpc,message,enum,table,store,handler,hook,deploy-target |
| `--service` | `string` | - | only this service's rpcs and handlers (ChatService, or Chat) |
| `--unimplemented` | `bool` | - | list the declared rpcs of forge-managed services that have no handler method, or only forge's unwired stub |

---

#### reliant forge project upgrade

Move the project to a newer forge and apply every mechanical fix for the range

Take the project from the forge it pins to a newer one, and do every mechanical
step on the way, so the gate is green without hand edits. In order:

  modules             go get / go mod tidy the forge requirement of each module
  codemods            the codemods keyed to the releases the range crosses
  templates           frozen-file drift (below)
  pin                 forge.yaml forge_version
  playbooks           the "automation:" steps of each crossed migration playbook
  vendored-contracts  re-vendor a contract forge.yaml upgrade.vendored_contracts
                      declares, when its lag report says it lags
  generate            forge generate
  locks               refresh lockfiles that disagree with their package.json
  lint                forge lint --json; what remains is reported, not fixed

--skip `<phase>` (repeatable) leaves a phase out so a big change can go in its own PR:
codemods (the registry codemods the range crosses), playbooks (the codemods a
playbook automates, connect-errors and composition-graph among them),
vendored-contracts, generate, locks, lint. modules, templates and pin always run.
The pin moves in the same run, so a skipped codemod is not picked up by the next
upgrade: the phase's detail names each one to run yourself ('forge fix `<name>`').

Whatever is left is printed as "Still needs a person" (--json: needs_human): a
lint error, a playbook whose old shape remains, a codemod that listed what it
could not decide. Rules still in their warn-first grace are listed under
"Gating soon" (--json: upcoming) with the release they gate from.

EXIT CODES (the contract a pin bot branches on)
  0  converged: every fix applied, nothing left for a person
  1  failed: an error stopped the run (generate failed, a codemod errored,
     bad flags). The run is not retried blindly: read the error
  2  could not determine: a package manager, registry or lint verdict could
     not be reached or read. Retryable
  9  needs a person: everything mechanical is applied; needs_human is not
     empty. Re-running changes nothing

--json prints exactly one document on stdout (schema forge-project-upgrade/v1:
ok, exit_code, error, mode, from, to, converged, phases, codemods, locks,
playbooks, vendored_contracts, needs_human, upcoming, template_drift); progress
goes to stderr. ok and exit_code are the process's own. It is also printed for
a failing run, so a bot can read what was applied before the failure.

Template drift: detect on frozen files (files written at 'forge project new' time but
not updated by 'forge generate') and apply updates from newer Forge templates.

Files that haven't been modified by the user are updated automatically.
User-modified files show a diff and are skipped unless --force is used.

--force is per-path. Naming paths after --force restricts the overwrite to
exactly those files, so you can adopt one new template without stomping every
other file you have customized. Bare --force keeps its whole-project meaning.

A file you have deliberately taken over is better claimed than defended:
'forge project disown `<path>` --reason ...' records the ownership transfer, and
upgrade then skips the file permanently — even under --force.

Scaffold-once files (each frontend's shared mechanism modules under src/lib and
src/hooks, plus the .github starters) get their own section and are NEVER written
implicitly. They are yours from birth; upgrade only reports what their templates
gained, how far behind you are in lines, and how many lines in your copy no
template accounts for. Adopt one by naming it:
'forge project upgrade --force `<path>`'. Bare --force does not reach them.

A scaffold-once file the project deleted stays deleted. The birth ledger
(.forge/scaffolded.json) records every scaffold-once file forge has written, so a
managed file the template ships that is absent AND recorded there — or declined
('forge project disown `<path>` --absent'), or disowned — is the project's decision,
not drift: upgrade never re-creates it (nor does 'forge generate'), and says so
under "deleted by you". A file forge has never written is still born, and recorded.
To have forge write a deleted one again: 'forge project rescaffold `<path>`'.

Upgrade synchronizes every project module that requires Forge using
GOWORK=off go get and go mod tidy, preserving module replacements and go.work.
If either command fails, its module-file changes are rolled back and upgrade
stops before changing templates or forge.yaml, with exact recovery commands.
After a successful upgrade the project's forge_version field in forge.yaml
is bumped to the current binary version (or to --to when provided). Migrations
whose declared version range AND detection script both match this project are
surfaced first, so the LLM running upgrade can follow them step-by-step.

--check SUMMARIZES; --check `<path>` DETAILS. The report names each file that
differs and sizes the difference in lines — it does NOT print diffs inline,
because on a real project that is thousands of lines nobody reads. Files are
grouped and ranked by what adopting them costs: the ones with no local edits
come first, since for those a single --force is the whole job. Name a path
after --check to see that one file's full diff, or pass --all to list every
file the groups summarized.

Examples:
  forge project upgrade                        # Upgrade to latest, run all needed migrations
  forge project upgrade --to 1.5.0             # Upgrade to a specific target version
  forge project upgrade --dry-run              # Show what would change (alias for --check)
  forge project upgrade --check                # Dry-run summary: what differs, and by how much
  forge project upgrade --check buf.yaml       # That one file's full diff
  forge project upgrade --check --all          # Every reported file (still no inline diffs)
  forge project upgrade --force                # Apply all updates, even for user-modified files
  forge project upgrade --force buf.yaml       # Overwrite ONLY buf.yaml; leave other edits alone
  forge project upgrade --json                 # What a pin bot runs; exit 0 means merge, 9 means hand to a person

```
reliant forge project upgrade [--force <path>...] [flags]
```

**Subcommands:**

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [`apply`](#reliant-forge-project-upgrade-apply) | Record a migration as applied (writes .forge/migrations.json) |
| [`list`](#reliant-forge-project-upgrade-list) | List pending forge migrations for this project |

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--all` | `bool` | - | List every reported file instead of the first few per group |
| `--allow-dirty` | `bool` | - | Run codemods that replace files you own even over uncommitted changes (they are otherwise left for you, and reported) |
| `--check` | `bool` | - | Dry-run: only show what would change, don't write files |
| `--dry-run` | `bool` | - | Alias for --check |
| `--force` | `bool` | - | Overwrite user-modified files. Name paths after it to overwrite only those |
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Print one JSON document (schema forge-project-upgrade/v1) on stdout; progress goes to stderr |
| `--skip` | `stringSlice` | `[]` | Phases not to run: codemods, playbooks, vendored-contracts, generate, locks, lint |
| `--to` | `string` | - | Target forge version (defaults to the current binary version) |

---

#### reliant forge project upgrade apply

Record a migration as applied (writes .forge/migrations.json)

Mark a migration as applied. This does NOT execute the migration —
loading the skill and running its steps is the user's (or an agent's)
job. 'apply' just records the outcome so later 'forge project upgrade list'
runs hide migrations that have already been worked through.

The migration ID is the release version it belongs to, which is also its
directory name under skills/forge/migrations/ (e.g. "v0.5.0").

```
reliant forge project upgrade apply <migration-id>
```

---

#### reliant forge project upgrade list

List pending forge migrations for this project

List forge migration skills for releases this project has not crossed
yet and whose detection script matches the project's current shape.

Migrations are LLM-readable playbooks under skills/forge/migrations/,
one per release that introduced a breaking change. This command does NOT
apply them — it surfaces the worklist so the user (or an agent) can load
each skill via 'forge skill load' and execute the steps. Use
'forge project upgrade apply `<id>`' to record a migration as applied once
you've finished its steps.

```
reliant forge project upgrade list [flags]
```

**Flags:**

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `--json` | `bool` | - | Output JSON instead of human-readable text |
